To prove the conclusion: assume the check returns true. The positivity conjuncts give
Nxy > 0 for all four settings, so no case split on Nxy = 0 is needed; that case cannot
arise. Cast the integer counts to R by IZR : Z → R, which preserves the inequalities.
Divide through by Nxy 2 (or N 4 , for condition 3), licensed by N > 0. After dividing,
                              xy                                 xy
the three inequalities are exactly conditions 1, 2, 3 of column-contractivity in R. The
Coq proof closes the post-division algebra by nra (nonlinear real arithmetic) acting
on a sum-of-squares-shaped goal.

   Plain reading. Three theorems snap together: the kernel step rule says no-trap
   means the integer check passed; the soundness lemma says a passing integer
   check means real-valued column-contractivity; the biconditional says column-
   contractivity means NPA-PSD. Stack them. A successful CHSH_LASSERT step
   is a proof that the witness-derived correlators land inside the NPA-PSD set. No
   physics input anywhere in the stack. The (1,0,1,0) correlation fails the integer
   check (condition 1: 1 − 1 − 1 = −1 < 0), so any program that accumulated those
   statistics traps on CHSH_LASSERT.


Theorem 18.7 (CHSH_LASSERT ⇒ NPA-PSD, chsh_lassert_no_trap_implie
s_npa_psd). Let s be a state and let s′ = vm_apply s (instr_chsh_lassert δ). If
s′ .vm_err = false and s′ .vm_pc = s.vm_pc + 1 (i.e., the CHSH_LASSERT step advanced

                                             150
The Thiele Machine                                           Devon Thiele • September 2026


normally rather than trapping), then the specified zero-marginal, zero-within-party-moment
matrix M built from the witness-derived correlators Exy of s.vm_witness is positive semi-
definite.

Proof. By the apply rule for instr_chsh_lassert: the step branches on column_
contractive_check_witness run on s.vm_witness. If the check returns false,
the step traps to LASSERT_TRAP_PC and sets vm_err = true. If the check returns
true, the step advances PC by 1 and leaves vm_err = false. By hypothesis, s′
exhibits the second behavior, so the integer check returned true on s.vm_witness.

Compose with the preceding theorem (integer-check soundness): the correlators Exy
derived from s.vm_witness satisfy the three real-valued column-contractivity condi-
tions.

Compose with the forward direction of column_contractive_iff_npa_psd (the
biconditional proved in Section 18): column-contractivity implies the NPA moment
matrix M is positive semidefinite.

Run the (1,0,1,0) correlation through CHSH_LASSERT and watch what the step does
with it: the integer check fails on the very first condition, the step refuses to advance,
and the state comes out the far side wearing vm_err = true. Nothing in the kernel
was ever told where (1,0,1,0) sits relative to any physics, and the point itself is not
unphysical: a classical mixture reaches it with room to spare, marginals and all. The
kernel was told to count, clear denominators, and test three inequalities, and for
this point the arithmetic simply would not close, because the point lies outside the
zero-marginal slice the three inequalities carve out. That is the only gate there is. A
trace whose correlators are column-contractive walks straight through; one whose
correlators are not gets stopped by its own numbers, and the kernel never has to know
why.

To falsify: exhibit E00 , E01 , E10 , E11 satisfying the three polynomial inequalities but
with a non-PSD NPA matrix. No such example exists under the stated definitions; the
theorem proves it impossible. Or exhibit a quantum experiment producing a non-PSD
NPA matrix. That would be news of a different kind. It would break the NPA bridge
or quantum mechanics, not this theorem.

18.7 Lifting the bridge to level 1+AB: the four-slice Schur cascade
Same test, bigger grid. The 5 × 5 grid only holds the four correlators. The 9 × 9 grid
also holds five higher moments, the ones that involve three or four measurements at
once. A bigger grid means more windows to check and a lot more arithmetic, so the
machine checks it in four stages, letting one more kind of moment move at each stage.
What follows is the formal layer.

Section 18 establishes the runtime bridge: CHSH_LASSERT implies PSD of a
fixed 5 × 5 moment matrix with zero marginals and zero within-party cross mo-

                                           151
The Thiele Machine                                               Devon Thiele • September 2026


ments. This fixed slice excludes some classical correlators. Allowing the within-
party moments to vary gives the full correlator elliptope developed in Section 18.8.
The Q1+AB checks instead validate a specified 9 × 9 moment matrix on the ba-
sis { I, A0 , A1 , B0 , B1 , A0 B0 , A0 B1 , A1 B0 , A1 B1 } with supplied higher moments. Their
soundness theorem establishes PSD for that matrix. For complete behaviors, in-
cluding marginals, finite-level NPA feasibility is a relaxation: Chaturvedi (2026,
arXiv:2607.14569) proves that no fixed finite level describes the whole quantum behav-
ior set in this scenario.

And the two levels are not two separate tricks. The 5 × 5 and the 9 × 9 checks are
one dimension-polymorphic PSD predicate evaluated at two sizes: top index 4 for
the 5 × 5, top index 8 for the 9 × 9, the same fold either way. The level-1 CHSH
biconditional from Section 18 is the dimension-5 instance of it, recovered as a corollary
that imports the original 5 × 5 result as a black box and adds no axioms over it (colum
n_contractive_iff_general_realizable). So the lift in this subsection is not
a 9-shaped coincidence dressed up as a method: it is the same realizability projection
run one dimension up, and the dimension-5 corollary reproducing the bridge you
already have is exactly the check that it is one predicate and not two.

18.7.1 The 9 × 9 moment matrix and its five γ parameters
The level-1+AB moment matrix is parametric in nine reals: the four CHSH correlators
( E00 , E01 , E10 , E11 ) and five additional moments

          γ1 = ⟨ A0 A1 B0 ⟩,         γ2 = ⟨ A0 A1 B1 ⟩,             γ3 = ⟨ A0 B0 B1 ⟩,
          γ4 = ⟨ A1 B0 B1 ⟩,         γ5 = ⟨ A0 A1 B0 B1 ⟩.

γ1 , γ2 label the two three-body A-A-B slots, γ3 , γ4 the two three-body A-B-B slots,
and γ5 the four-body slot. γ5 enters the matrix in two cells that are not equal: +γ5
in the ( A0 B0 , A1 B1 ) cell and −γ5 in the conjugate ( A0 B1 , A1 B0 ) cell. Here is where
that convention comes from. In the physics picture those two cells hold the same four
operators with B0 and B1 swapped, and orthogonal measurements anticommute, so
the swap flips the sign. That minus sign is the anticommutator a CHSH violation runs
on. That is the motivation, and only the motivation. In Coq the angle brackets are
labels on the entries of a real polynomial matrix. The nine parameters are independent
real slots, the opposite signs are part of how the matrix is defined, and nothing in the
proofs builds operators, adjoints, or anticommutation. Reading the entries as physical
expectation values would take a separate representation theorem. The 9 × 9 entries
are polynomials of degree ≤ 2 in ( Eij , γk ), and PSD of this matrix is the formal Q1+AB
condition used below.

18.7.2 Why four slices
What a slice is. Here “slice” means three things together: a parameter domain, the
PSD predicate on it, and the integer check that certifies it. What the source proves is
that each check is sound. It does not prove that the accepted sets nest strictly, that the


                                             152
The Thiele Machine                                            Devon Thiele • September 2026


integer checker accepts every PSD point in its domain, or that Slice D is the whole
physical quantum cone. Any inclusion claim below is about the formal domains only,
and would need its own zero-extension and certificate-translation proof.

The substrate certifies four γ-slices, each via its own cert-opcode and its own integer-
arithmetic check:

   • Slice A (γ = 0, tag 0x2F): all five γk pinned to zero. The bool decider checks Q1
     column-contractivity (the three conditions of §18) plus the SOS sum-of-squares
                2 + E2 + E2 + E2 ≤ 1. Closed by an explicit 4-D Cauchy-Schwarz
     condition E00     01    10      11
     SOS identity.
   • Slice B (γ5 free, tag 0x30): the four-body moment γ5 is admitted as a bucket-pair
     argument; the other four γ are zero. The check adds a weighted 4-D Cauchy-
     Schwarz identity that handles the 2γ5 (v11 v22 − v12 v21 ) cross term arising in the
     residual (the vij are the coordinates of the paired 4-D vectors the identity runs
     over) (the relative minus is the conjugate-cell sign above: γ5 on the v11 v22 pairing,
     −γ5 on the v12 v21 pairing).
   • Slice C (γ3 , γ4 , γ5 free, tag 0x31): the two A-B-B moments and the four-body
     moment are admitted; γ1 = γ2 = 0. The check is a 4 × 4 Sylvester PD test on
     a difference matrix Hγ345 = det M · M M − M N , where M M and M N are the two
     denominator-cleared moment blocks the slice compares and det M is the cleared
     determinant scaling them, derived via an augmented 2 × 2 Schur-slack lemma.
   • Slice D (full γ1 , . . . , γ5 free, tag 0x32): all five γ parameters are admitted. The
     check is a compositional sym6 → sym5 → sym4 Schur cascade: the only check
     stiff enough to handle the bilinear A-A-B cross terms γ1 , γ2 introduce in the
     residual.

These four are not four independent gadgets. Their parameter domains nest, A ⊂ B ⊂
C ⊂ D: each slice stops pinning a moment the previous slice held at zero. The reason
to build them in exactly this order is that every newly freed moment drags a harder
cross term into the residual, and you would rather meet those one at a time than all at
once. What climbs is the set of parameters each check lets in. Whether the accepted
sets climb strictly is a different question, and nothing here proves it. Each check is
proved sound in one direction, into PSD of the specified matrix; none has a proved
converse. Slice D, with nothing pinned, is the broadest check here. It is not a theorem
that Slice D is the whole Q1+AB cone.




                                           153
The Thiele Machine                                                        Devon Thiele • September 2026


18.7.3 The compositional Schur cascade (Slice D)
The mathematical core of Slice D is the chain

  PSD9( M) ⇐ sym6 PD-interior on the 21 entries of M6×6
             ⇐ sym5 PD-interior on the 5 × 5 Schur complement of row 1
                        ⇐ sym4 PD-interior on the 4 × 4 Schur complement of row 2



                                      PSD9 ( M)      the goal

                                                  21 H bridges
                                                  (lift cleared integers → reals)


                              sym6 PD-interior 21 entries of M6×6

                                                  15 S6 bridges


                        sym5 PD-interior 5×5 Schur complement of row 1

                                                  10 S5 bridges


                        sym4 PD-interior 4×4 Schur complement of row 2

                                                  4 sym4 leading-minor bridges


                           Slice C lemma sym4 PD-interior, base case


Figure 9: The Schur cascade lifts the base-case sym4 PD-interior fact from Slice C all the
way up to PSD9 ( M ). Each arrow is one Schur-complement reduction, a polynomial
identity in the correlators and moments. The counts beside each arrow are how many
such identities the integer check grinds through at that step, one per matrix entry.

where M6×6 is what the 9 × 9 collapses to once the Q1+AB subsumption identities pin
its dependent entries: the projection onto the basis {v B1 , v B2 , v11 , v12 , v21 , v22 }, after
which the 9 × 9 lands in the PSD cone whenever the 6 × 6 residual does. From there it
is Schur complements all the way down, and it is the same move you already watched
at level 1, run on a bigger matrix. The Schur-complement criterion (Section A), the one
the level-1 bridge used on its 4 × 4 block, turns a positive-definiteness question about
the 6 × 6 into one about a 5 × 5 Schur complement; run it again and the 5 × 5 becomes
a 4 × 4; and the 4 × 4 closes by Sylvester’s criterion, all four leading principal minors
positive. That is the whole soundness argument: a base case and three reductions,
each one a Schur step you can carry out by hand, stacked from the 4 × 4 back up to
PSD9.

Every reduction is a single polynomial identity in the correlators Eij and the moments
γk . To keep the opcode’s check decidable and integer-only, each identity is multiplied

                                               154
The Thiele Machine                                           Devon Thiele • September 2026


                                                                                      2
through by the common denominator KZ = N00 N01 N10 N11 · Dg1 Dg2 Dg3 Dg4 Dg5 ,
where the Nij are the per-setting trial counts and the Dgk the γ-bucket denominators,
and verified on integers, the same clear-the-denominators move as level 1. It is a lot of
arithmetic: one identity per matrix entry, twenty-one across the 6 × 6, fifteen for the
first Schur step, ten for the second, four leading minors at the base. The kernel grinds
every one. But the grinding isn’t the argument; the argument is the iterated Schur
reduction above, and a reader who wants to break it breaks the reduction, not the fifty
identities. A passing integer check is those reductions holding, and those reductions
holding is M in the PSD cone. The check leans on nothing past the background axioms
level 1 already leaned on: the standard library’s classical-reals decidability axioms
(sig_forall_dec, sig_not_dec) and functional extensionality.

18.7.4 What this delivers and what it does not
Soundness. For each of the four Q1+AB assertion opcodes, the successful-step condi-
tion in its theorem implies symmetry and positive semidefiniteness of the specified
9 × 9 moment matrix at the witness-derived correlators and supplied γ values. This is
an arithmetic certificate about those entries. The theorem does not construct a physical
experiment.

Scope. These theorems establish the four integer-check soundness implications and
the associated matrix identities. Physical realization, completeness of the checks, and
identification of a moment relaxation with a quantum behavior set require their own
arguments. The full correlator elliptope is treated separately below.

Accounting. Each assertion instruction has declared cost S(mu_delta) ≥ 1, charged
on both success and failure. The integer test establishes its matrix property; the cost
schedule records payment for executing the test. These are separate guarantees. The
assertion opcodes preserve vm_certified; their success is observed through the
stated trap and error conditions.

Falsification. Exhibit witness counters and γ bucket pairs for which one of the four
cert-opcodes does not trap but the resulting 9 × 9 NPA moment matrix is not PSD,
OR show that the Schur-cascade soundness theorem at any slice is incorrect (an SOS
identity wrong, a minors-witness step that does not imply PSD9, a bool decider un-
sound), OR show that the soundness chain depends on a project-local axiom beyond
the two standard-library axioms cited above. The Coq Print Assumptions report
is the audit surface: every theorem either lists no new axiom or fails the proof gate.

18.8 The full correlator set: the elliptope completion
The slice nails two numbers in the grid to zero. The elliptope lets them move. For the
slice you ask whether the point fits one fixed grid. For the elliptope you ask whether
there’s any way to fill in those two blanks that makes the grid pass.

The fixed orthogonal completion excludes some classical correlators, including every
deterministic local strategy. The full elliptope allows the within-party cross moments


                                           155
The Thiele Machine                                                   Devon Thiele • September 2026


to vary. ElliptopeCompletion.v defines membership by existence of a PSD com-
pletion and proves inclusion of classical correlators, the Tsirelson bound, and exclusion
of the PR box. That is the mathematical object this subsection examines.

The characterization is the one Tsirelson’s theorem forces. Take the same 5 × 5 moment
matrix, marginals still zero, but stop pinning the two cross moments: let x stand for
⟨ A0 A1 ⟩ and y for ⟨ B0 B1 ⟩, both free. Call a correlator tuple ( E00 , E01 , E10 , E11 ) elliptope-
realizable when some completion ( x, y) makes the matrix PSD (elliptope_realiza
ble, defined in ElliptopeCompletion.v). The existential over ( x, y) is the entire
difference between the slice and the set: the slice asks whether the point is coherent
with one fixed completion, the zero one; the set asks whether any completion works.

Five theorems pin the characterization down, all mechanised, all with Print
Assumptions reporting only the same stdlib families as the rest of the CHSH al-
gebra (the classical-reals construction and functional extensionality; zero project-local
axioms):

   • deterministic_strategy_elliptope: every deterministic local strategy
     is in the set. The witness is the honest one, x = a0 a1 and y = b0 b1 , the cross
     moments the strategy actually has, and the completed matrix becomes an explicit
     Gram matrix whose quadratic form is a sum of two squares. All sixteen sign
     patterns are instances of one parametric theorem. classical_tightness_
     witness_elliptope names the sharpest instance: the all-ones strategy, the
     µ = 0 witness of classical_bound_achieved that the slice gate traps, is
     elliptope-realizable.
   • turing_point_elliptope: (1, 0, 1, 0), this document’s running example of
     a classically reachable point the slice rejects, is in the set, witnessed by x = 1,
     y = 0 and the sum-of-squares decomposition v20 + (v1 + v2 + v3 )2 + v24 of its
     completed quadratic form.
   • elliptope_convex: the set is closed under binary convex combination; elli
     ptope_finite_mixture extends that to arbitrary finite sub-convex mixtures
     by a mechanised renormalization induction (the weight deficit absorbed by the
     origin, elliptope_zero); and lhv_mixture_elliptope composes it with
     the deterministic theorem: every finite mixture of deterministic sign strategies,
     which is to say every local-hidden-variable correlator, lands inside. Not by hand-
     waving from the binary case; the n-ary theorem is in the kernel. As a small check
     on the geometry, the classical point (1, 0, 1, 0) has row squared norms 12 + 02 = 1
     and 12 + 02 = 1 if the rows are ordered as ( E00 , E01 ) and ( E10 , E11 ), while its
     columns have squared norms 12 + 12 = 2 and 02 + 02 = 0. Transposing the
     matrix supplies the corresponding column/row counterexample; the distinc-
     tion matters because the fixed-completion proof uses E T E and therefore column
     contractivity.
                                                                                      √
   • elliptope_tsirelson: everything in the set satisfies S2 ≤ 8, i.e. |S| ≤ 2 2.

                                                156
The Thiele Machine                                            Devon Thiele • September 2026


      The proof is worth a sentence, because the fixed-slice column bound does not
      hold for arbitrary completions. At x ̸= 0 the column-norm bound fails, and
                                                                                     √
      honestly so: the point (1, 0, 1, 0), completed at x = 1, has a column of norm 2.
      What replaces it is Cauchy–Schwarz run inside the PSD form itself (psd_cau
      chy_schwarz, derived from the same discriminant lemma the reverse bridge
      uses): the two budgets ∥ B0 + B1 ∥2 = 2 + 2y and ∥ B0 − B1 ∥2 = 2 − 2y are coupled
      through the completion, their product is at most 4, and S2 ≤ 8 falls out of that
      coupling. The bound holds at every point of the set, not because each column is
      short, but because the completion cannot make both budgets long at once.
   • pr_box_not_elliptope: the PR box has no completion; its S is 4 and the
     Tsirelson theorem refuses it. And beyond_classical_elliptope exhibits
     a rational point with S = 12/5 > 2 inside the set, so the containment of the
     classical polytope is strict: classical ⊊ elliptope, and the PR box lies outside it,
     from inside the formalization. That the elliptope sits inside the no-signaling set
     is not stated as a theorem here.

The correlator geometry has three distinct sets: the classical polytope and the zero-
marginal slice intersect without containment, and both sit inside the elliptope, which
admits every honest classical point the slice refuses while still refusing everything
      √
past 2 2.

The completion gate is executable. ElliptopeGate.v builds the decidable check
an opcode can branch on (elliptope_check_full), in the same integer-only disci-
pline as column_contractive_check_witness: no real and no rational evaluated
at runtime, every denominator cleared into Z. The check has two branches. The PD
branch is the cheap path for a strictly-interior point: it takes completion buckets for
( x, y), exactly the bucket-pair idiom the 1+AB opcodes use for their γ moments, and
runs the fraction-free Sylvester test on the denominator-cleared completed matrix,
whose soundness is the same sym4_qf_nonneg_from_pd LDLT lemma the Slice-C
development proves. The LDL branch checks certificates that can include singular
boundary completions: it takes a rational LDLT certificate, six subdiagonal entries
and four pivots over common positive denominators, and verifies the ten entry equa-
tions of M = LDLT in cross-multiplied Z-arithmetic. Its soundness (elliptope
_ldl_check_sound) is a sum-of-weighted-squares identity: given the equations,
the quadratic form is d1 P12 + d2 P22 + d3 P32 + d4 P42 . The file proves soundness of this
certificate format and supplies concrete accepted boundary points. The load-bearing
theorem is elliptope_check_full_sound: a passing check entails elliptope mem-
bership of the witness-derived correlators, with the check’s own passing as its only
hypothesis. And the gate computes, not just proves, each by reflexivity: gate_
accepts_all_ones runs the µ = 0 tightness witness through the LDL branch (its
all-ones completion is rank-one singular) and it passes; gate_accepts_turing_p
oint does the same for (1, 0, 1, 0); gate_accepts_beyond_classical passes the
interior S = 12/5 point through the PD branch; gate_accepts_pythagorean_b


                                           157
The Thiele Machine                                         Devon Thiele • September 2026


oundary certifies (3/5, 4/5, 4/5, −3/5), whose arcsine sum is exactly π by the 3-4-5
triangle so it sits exactly on the Tsirelson curve with S = 14/5 > 2; it’s invisible to
the strict PD branch because its completion is singular, and it’s reached by the LDL
branch with pivots (1, 1, 0, 0) at x = y = 0; and elliptope_full_gate_never_
accepts_pr_box proves no completion buckets and no certificate ever get the PR
box through, not by enumeration but by soundness plus the Tsirelson theorem closing
both branches at once. This certificate format needs rational completion data. Sound-
ness and the accepted examples don’t show the gate accepts every elliptope point, and
they don’t rule out other exact certificate formats.

The runtime CHSH_LASSERT opcode checks the fixed orthogonal slice. The elliptope
gate is not an opcode. Making it one would take instruction semantics, a cost rule, and
a hardware implementation. The completion results are about correlators. They don’t
pin down the full set of behaviors once marginals come in. Calling the completion
model physical quantum correlations leans on outside mathematics, and producing
entanglement in hardware would take physical evidence. The direct mathematical
challenges are concrete: a deterministic sign pattern whose completed Gram matrix
has a negative quadratic form would kill deterministic_strategy_elliptope;
an elliptope-realizable tuple with S2 > 8 would kill elliptope_tsirelson; a PSD
completion for the PR box would kill pr_box_not_elliptope and Tsirelson with
it.

19 Irreversibility accounting and the µ-hierarchy

19.1 The Landauer association is motivation only
Same shape as Landauer’s 1961 argument: erasing one bit must release at least kT ln 2
of heat into the environment (k Boltzmann, T absolute temperature, ln 2 the natural
log of 2). The Coq proofs below do not depend on Landauer being right about the
physics. They formalise a conservative accounting indicator at the instruction level:
a cost-positive instruction contributes one to irreversible_bits by definition.
That label is bookkeeping, not a proof that the instruction erased information or was
physically irreversible. The count of positively charged firings is bounded by total µ.
Shape borrowed, content paid for separately.

   Plain reading. Each cost-positive instruction contributes at least 1 to total cost
   and exactly 1 to the indicator. Sum across the trace and the count is bounded
   by the cost. A 0/1 indicator bounded by what it indicates. All it shares with
   Landauer is the shape: a lower-bound quantity attached to an event. It is not an
   irreversibility theorem. No physics in the proof. No microscopic erasure model.
   No physical entropy. No Boltzmann constant. The conversion of µ counts to joules
   is a calibration premise (mu_landauer_calibration, used by calibrated_
   mu_landauer_energy; Section 25). The separate Unruh–Clausius flux bridge
   mu_landauer_unruh_calibrated is likewise a premise. No kernel theorem



                                         158
The Thiele Machine                                           Devon Thiele • September 2026


    proves either; theorems in NoFIToEinstein.v take the second as an explicit
    hypothesis.


Theorem 19.1 (µ-Landauer step bound). Let irreversible_bits(i ) be the indi-
cator function that returns 1 if instruction_cost(i ) > 0 and 0 otherwise. Let
total_irreversible_bits(trace) := ∑i∈trace irreversible_bits(i ). Then for
every trace,

   total_irreversible_bits(trace) ≤ total_instruction_cost(trace).

The Coq witness is total_irreversible_bits_le_cost.                    The same sum is
trace_total_cost in MuInitiality.v.

Proof. Per-instruction inequality: for every i,          irreversible_bits(i )         ≤
instruction_cost(i ). Two cases.

Case instruction_cost(i ) = 0. Then irreversible_bits(i ) = 0 by definition,
and 0 ≤ 0 holds.

Case instruction_cost(i ) > 0. Then irreversible_bits(i ) = 1, and the cost
schedule (Section 7) gives instruction_cost(i ) ≥ S(δ) ≥ 1 for cert-setters or
instruction_cost(i ) ≥ δ ≥ 1 otherwise. So 1 ≤ instruction_cost(i ).

Summing the per-instruction inequality over the trace:


    ∑ irreversible_bits(i) ≤ ∑ instruction_cost(i)
  i ∈trace                              i ∈trace

                                                     = trace_total_cost(trace).

The right equality is by definition of trace_total_cost (or via the Latent-µ theo-
rem applied to the trace from µ0 = 0).

The physical claim has to pass a consistency test first. calibrated_model_exists
_iff_positive_cost says exactly when the abstract positive-dissipation premise
and the upper-calibration premise can hold together. The explicit yes/no reset model
has an instance. But keep the previous state in a history list and every fixed-instruction
step becomes injective, while the step you can see stays the same. zero_cost_v
m_jump_has_injective_history_lift applies that move to the zero-cost F1
counterexample. The PC can collapse to one place while the bigger state keeps its past.
I can’t spot physical erasure by watching the PC alone.

The physical boundary. Counting positively charged instructions does not give the
formal charge a physical interpretation. Neither does the finite-map Python demon-
stration: it checks its combinatorial model, not heat released by a device. A physical



                                            159
The Thiele Machine                                               Devon Thiele • September 2026


model has to use jointly satisfiable premises, account for retained history, and jus-
tify its connection to an experiment. The permanent-certificate theorem (Section 9)
supplies the first two for the certifying step. Its premises hold together, as the reset
model shows, and finite memory accounts for retained history. The entropy version
carries it as far as a formula for heat, k B T ln((m + k )/m), with Landauer’s principle
as the named premise. The VM ledger has no measured energy price without the
experimental correspondence.

The calorimeter protocol makes that boundary exact. CalorimeterProtocol.v
takes a two-state register with energies 0 and ∆, excited-state population p, and the
discrete master equation

                             p′ = p + dt k01 (1 − p) − k10 p .
                                                            


For the canonical reset p = 1/2, p′ = 0, dt = 1, k01 = 0, and k10 = 1, the equation
closes and the fixed-Hamiltonian bath heat is exactly ∆/2 (canonical_reset_he
at_exact). Choosing ∆ = 2k B T ln 2 makes that heat exactly k B T ln 2 (selected_
gap_gives_landauer_heat). That equality is a selected Hamiltonian scale, not
a derivation of it. The same populations and rates with ∆ = k B T ln 2 give half the
Landauer value, and master_equation_does_not_fix_heat_scale exhibits
identical population dynamics with different heat. The protocol counts one logical
µ event, and that count equals what CERTIFY 0 charges from the clean state, with
no theorem mapping the protocol’s register states to VM states (vm_minimal_ce
rtification_charges_canonical_reset_mu), and given an uncertified state,
no VM step switches certification on for less (vm_certification_charges_a
t_least_canonical_reset_mu). A device test must therefore measure the gap,
populations, rates, and bath heat independently, and must separately establish thermal
admissibility, detailed balance, and that the device’s physical reset is that VM event.
The formal protocol is an executable experimental specification; it is not a measured
device or an unconditional conversion of µ to joules.

What the permanence results add to Landauer, and what they don’t. I went looking
for a prediction the permanent-certificate theorems make that Landauer’s principle
alone doesn’t. There isn’t one. The smallest case, one certified state and one flipping
in, is setting a bit to one whatever it held. That is the bit reset Landauer described, and
Bérut and colleagues measured its heat approaching k B T ln 2 in a colloidal double-well
experiment (Nature 483, 2012). The general bound, k B T ln((m + k )/m) for squeezing
m + k equally likely states into m, is Landauer’s formula for that squeeze. The split
between a known state, which owes nothing, and an uncertain one, which owes heat,
is Maroney’s and Sagawa’s. So the physics content is exactly Landauer’s principle
plus one bridge: on finite memory, a certificate that lasts can only be switched on by a
squeeze. The bridge formalized here is checked. The heat is Landauer’s, and Bérut’s
experiment already measured it for the smallest case. A test of the bridge itself would
need a device that stores a certificate with no step able to revoke it and a way to meter

                                           160
The Thiele Machine                                                  Devon Thiele • September 2026


the certifying step alone, and I don’t know of one.

19.2 The µ-hierarchy
Borrow the silhouette of the classical time-hierarchy theorem, and then say honestly
how much smaller the thing inside it is. The shape is the part everyone knows: more
of the resource buys you something you provably could not have had with less. The
resource here is µ, and the something is a certification level: traces are classified by an
executed CERTIFY whose declared charge is at least k. Every level k ≥ 1 is reachable
by a one-instruction witness that pays exactly k, and no trace in that level can cost less
than k. That pairing, reachability on one side and a conservation floor on the other, is
the whole content. It is not a statement that one problem class outruns another, and
no complexity-class separation is claimed.

   Plain reading. Two parts. Part (1) is by construction: use the one-instruction trace
   CERTIFY(k − 1). Its charge is S(k − 1) = k, it ends certified, and there is no
   padding instruction. Part (2) is the trace-side lower bound: the executed level-k
   CERTIFY appears in the ledger with cost at least k, so the total cost is at least k.
   For the smallest nontrivial example, k = 2 is exactly CERTIFY(1), which costs
   S(1) = 2.


Theorem 19.2 (µ-Hierarchy (mu_hierarchy_theorem)). Call a trace level-k certified
when it ends with vm_certified = true and its executed-instruction log contains a
CERTIFY whose charge S(δ) is at least k. For every k ≥ 1:

   1. There exists a level-k certified trace with total cost exactly k.
   2. Any level-k certified trace has trace_total_cost ≥ k.

Proof. Part (1): the level-k witness. The one-instruction trace [instr_certify (k −
1)] does it: the single CERTIFY charges S(k − 1) = k, which certifies at level k by
definition, sets vm_certified to true, and is the whole bill. Total cost exactly k.

Part (2): cost lower bound. The level-k hypothesis hands over an executed CERTIFY
with S(δ) ≥ k; the kernel proof finds it by scanning the executed-instruction log. That
one step charged at least k to the ledger, every other step charged at least 0, and by
Latent µ the total cost is the sum of the step charges, so the total is at least k. The scan
earns the floor from the certifying step itself.

Each level k ≥ 1 is therefore realizable at cost exactly k, and no trace certifies at level k
for less.

   Plain reading. For any budget you propose, the hierarchy theorem produces a
   certified trace that exceeds it by 1. So no finite budget caps all certifications.




                                                161
The Thiele Machine                                              Devon Thiele • September 2026


Corollary 19.3 (mu_hierarchy_no_upper_bound). No fixed µ-budget suffices for all
certification levels.

Proof. Suppose for contradiction that some fixed budget B ≥ 0 suffices for all certifica-
tion levels, meaning every certified trace satisfies vm_mu ≤ B. Apply the µ-Hierarchy
theorem at k = B + 1: there exists a certified trace with vm_mu = B + 1 > B, contra-
dicting the budget bound.

What this comes down to is that the ledger does not give discounts. Pick any integer
level you like and there is a certified trace that arrives at exactly that level; try to reach
it for one unit less and you cannot, not because anybody forbade it but because the
cost was already spent on the way up and conservation will not let you unspend it.
That is the entire argument, and where it is mechanised the check agrees. Notice what
I did not say. I did not say more µ makes the substrate “strictly more powerful”. That
phrase belongs to the problem-class reading, the question of whether a bigger budget
cracks problems a smaller one cannot, and this theorem does not touch it. I would
rather leave that fence standing in plain sight than collect on a claim I have not paid
for.

Here is the classical statement the shape comes from, so you can see exactly where
the resemblance starts and where it stops. The time-hierarchy theorem says that for a
time-constructible bound T (n) there are problems you can solve in time T (n) log T (n)
that you cannot solve in time T (n): hand the machine a little more clock and it can
decide things it could not decide before. That is a statement about which problems
are in reach. The µ-hierarchy keeps the silhouette and trades the cargo: more µ buys
more certification levels, which is a statement about how high the ledger can climb,
not about which problems come into reach. It does not use the classical theorem, and
it does not define a single time-bounded class. So treat the resemblance as a way to
see the result, not as a smuggled inheritance of the classical theorem’s strength. The
proof a few lines up is the part actually holding weight; the analogy is just the light I
am reading it by.

A direct counterexample would be a k ≥ 1 trace reaching vm_certified = true
and vm_mu ≥ k with total trace cost < k. Under the theorem’s premises that cannot
happen; if it does, the cost accounting or the proof is wrong.

20 Discrete Geometry over Partition Graphs

20.1 Discrete triangulation of the partition graph
A partition graph is not only a bookkeeping structure. You can also read it as a shape,
a discrete geometric object, and put geometric questions to it. The honesty of the
framing matters here, so I will be plain about what is and is not being claimed. The
result below reads the partition graph as a triangulated combinatorial object, a graph
cut into triangular pieces, and sets it in an equilateral angle model: every corner of


                                             162
The Thiele Machine                                               Devon Thiele • September 2026


every triangle contributes π/3 radians, which is 60 degrees, because the three angles
of an equilateral triangle are equal and a full turn is 2π radians, or 360 degrees. Those
angles are a modeling choice. They are not pulled out of µ costs, and I am not going
to dress them up as if they were.

A discrete triangulation defines a curvature-like angle defect. As I understand it (I
researched this for the project, so check me on the geometry): on a flat surface, if you
draw a triangle, the interior angles sum to 180◦ . On a curved surface, the angles can
sum to more or less than 180◦ . Curvature is the amount by which triangles deviate
from flat. For an interior vertex of a triangulated surface, the usual discrete curvature is
2π minus the incident angle sum. At a boundary vertex, the corresponding boundary
turning contribution uses π instead. The quantity used in the Coq result below uses
2π at every vertex; on a surface with boundary it therefore includes an extra boundary
contribution.

I went looking for the right invariant and found the Euler characteristic χ, a number
that captures the overall shape of a triangulated surface, independent of how you
draw the triangles. For any triangulation with V vertices, E edges, and F triangular
faces: χ = V − E + F. As I read it, no matter how you triangulate the same surface,
you get the same number. For a sphere, χ = 2. For a torus (donut shape), χ = 0. The
continuum Gauss-Bonnet theorem (which I am citing, not certifying) says the total
curvature of a closed surface equals 2πχ: the local geometric information (curvature)
integrates to a global topological fact (shape). The discrete analogue is stated below.
The continuum result is exposition.

20.2 The restricted angle-defect identity
The definition well_formed_triangulated bundles properties of a partition
graph, including triangle and incidence conditions and the additional equation B = 3χ.
That equation is a hypothesis, not a general theorem about triangulated disks. For
example, a disk made by dividing a quadrilateral into two triangles has V = 4, E = 5,
F = 2, B = 4, and χ = 1, so it fails this extra condition. The predicate does not
separately construct a planar embedding or establish every topological property of a
disk.

   Plain reading. The 5π comes from two places: how D is defined, and the extra
   boundary-count rule B = 3χ. Coq checks what follows from them. It doesn’t show
   that rule is forced by computation, by topology in general, or by nature. Take
   B = 3χ away and what’s left is D = 2πχ + πB under the incidence equations.
   There’s no universal 5πχ law.


Theorem 20.1 (Restricted angle-defect identity (discrete_gauss_bonnet)). Let g
be a partition graph satisfying well_formed_triangulated. In addition to its graph
and triangle conditions, this predicate requires 3F = 2I + B, the triangle-incidence degree sum
∑v deg(v) = 3F, and B = 3χ, with χ = V − E + F. The edge definitions give E = I + B.

                                             163
The Thiele Machine                                           Devon Thiele • September 2026


Assign angle π/3 to every triangle corner and define

                             D ( g) = ∑ 2π − deg(v)π/3 ,
                                                      
                                       v

including the same 2π term at boundary vertices. Then D ( g) = 5πχ( g).

Proof. The required triangle-incidence sum gives D = 2πV − πF. From E = I + B
and 3F = 2I + B, it follows that B = 2E − 3F. Consequently,

           D − 2πχ = (2πV − πF ) − 2π (V − E + F ) = π (2E − 3F ) = πB.

The additional hypothesis B = 3χ now gives D = 2πχ + πB = 5πχ.

For a triangulated surface whose boundary is a union of simple cycles, the number
of boundary vertices equals B. Replacing 2π by π at those boundary vertices sub-
tracts πB, giving the usual combined interior-curvature and boundary-turning total
2πχ. This explains the relation to discrete Gauss–Bonnet. It requires identifying the
boundary vertices and the appropriate surface hypotheses. The existing Coq theo-
rem proves the defined D identity under its own predicate; it does not formalize that
boundary-corrected theorem.

For a simple equilateral triangle, the distinction is visible without any machinery.
Summing 2π − π/3 at its three vertices gives 5π. Summing the boundary terms π −
π/3 gives 2π. The first sum is the quantity defined here; the second is the boundary
turning total. Both calculations can be right while answering different questions.

I did not write these proofs by hand. I directed LLMs and used the checker to test the
formal statements. That method still leaves me responsible for checking the definitions
and the interpretation. The 5π identity is a conditional combinatorial result. It isn’t
evidence for a new physical constant, and it doesn’t derive angles from µ cost.



Part III

Checking it

21 What Coq is, and why I used it
Coq is a proof assistant, which is a phrase I had to go look up. Underneath the phrase
it is a programming language, but one whose type system is strict enough to do a
thing ordinary languages cannot: you write a proof down as a program, and the
compiler refuses it unless the program genuinely matches the statement you claimed.
You cannot talk it into a maybe.



                                           164
The Thiele Machine                                          Devon Thiele • September 2026


Here is the idea. In a normal programming language, a type says something about
what kind of value a variable holds. In Python, x: int means x is an integer. In
Coq, types are propositions. The type n > 0 means “the proof that n is positive.” A
function from n > 0 to n + 1 > 0 is a proof that if n is positive, n + 1 is too.

The compiler checks whether the types line up. If your proof has a hole and you write
“trust me,” it rejects you. You can’t bluff a proof term past it. But here is the part
people skip, and it’s the part that matters: the compiler checks that the proof matches
the statement. It does not check that the statement says what you meant. Hand it a
statement that means nothing and it’ll prove it for you, three characters, no complaint.
So the compiler accepting a proof is not the same as the thing being true. It means this
proof matches this statement, and whether that’s the right statement is on me, not the
machine.

So I didn’t use Coq to make anything true. I used it because I don’t trust my own
eye to catch a gap in an argument I want to believe. I spent months convinced by my
own reasoning, pushed the proof through, and watched a key step come apart in my
hands. The machine caught where I’d fooled myself. That is what it’s for, and it’s the
same source the running machine and the chip get extracted from. Gap-catcher and
extractor. Not the proof. The proof is the argument, and an argument has to close in a
way a person can follow, or it hasn’t closed at all.

21.1 Reproducing the source and proof contracts
Repository: https://github.com/sethirus/The-Thiele-Machine.                      The
repository carries the formal sources, pinned dependency trees, executable contract
probes, and the native reproduction runner. The authoritative reproduction procedure
is documented in docs/REPRODUCTION.md; it builds a fresh source snapshot and
does not rely on patches or precompiled proof objects.

Use the native toolchain required by the repository, then run the following commands
from the repository root:

python3 scripts/reproduce_coq.py --jobs 1
python3 scripts/check_coq_probe.py
bash monograph/build_monograph.sh

The source-only reproduction has to exit zero. Don’t stop at the exit status, though.
Look at the theorem types and their global dependencies too, because a closed report
doesn’t make a theorem’s own premises go away. The monograph build regenerates
the publication outputs from the TeX sources. The reproduction records it writes are
evidence for that one run. They aren’t source files.

RTL generation is a separate command, bash scripts/kami_extract.sh, requir-
ing the Bluespec compiler and its standard library in addition to the Coq/OCaml
toolchain. Its logs, generated Verilog, synthesis report, and any bitstream have to



                                          165
The Thiele Machine                                          Devon Thiele • September 2026


name the source they were actually built from. A passing Coq build doesn’t give you
a fresh synthesis or timing measurement. The proof-audit output also records the
source state and date it describes.

21.2 The Inquisitor
Beyond Coq’s type checker, the project has a custom proof auditor called the Inquisitor,
shipped with the proof corpus. It runs on every commit and emits 87 distinct rule
codes. The main categories:

   • No holes. No Admitted, admit, or give_up anywhere in the active proof files.
     No unproved Axiom or Parameter declarations in project code.
   • Vacuity heuristics. No theorem whose conclusion is literally True, 0 = 0, or
     any other tautology that proves nothing. No “theorem” of the form P → P.
     Existence claims backed by a constant witness get flagged. A flag is a reason
     to look, not a verdict: some constructions, like a fixed interpreter program, are
     supposed to be fixed, and they get judged on their premises and on what they
     actually compute. Coq’s kernel already prevents circularly dependent proofs
     (it checks definitional well-foundedness); the Inquisitor catches separately the
     case where a proof is technically valid but epistemically empty, proving True
     by trivial, for instance.
   • Physics-definition heuristics. No physics quantity defined as a constant place-
     holder. No physics claim without a corresponding invariance condition. No
     theorem stated about a physics concept if that concept is not genuinely formal-
     ized.
   • No trivial true proofs. If a theorem’s entire proof is trivial or tauto and the
     conclusion reduces to True, the theorem is rejected.
   • Scope enforcement. The proof tree is split into tiers. Tier 1 is the kernel files:
     the core proof of the machine. Tier 1 files may not import from exploratory
     or research-side files. Theorem connectivity: every file in the active proof tree
     must reach the cost or semantics foundation through its import chain. If a file is
     disconnected, it does not get to claim the kernel’s pedigree.
   • Comment hygiene. The maintained source and documentation comments con-
     tain no unfinished or historical review markers; the repository checks this with
     scripts/comment_hygiene.py alongside the Coq proof audit.

The proof-audit output records the source state it was generated from, so regenerate
it whenever the source changes. Its rules are heuristics. They point at places to look.
They don’t prove a theorem means what it should, and they can’t promise every empty
premise got caught.




                                          166
The Thiele Machine                                         Devon Thiele • September 2026



22 Zero Admitted: what it actually means
“Zero Admitted” is literal. Nowhere in the proof tree did I write “trust me” and walk
on. Every step the checker could see, it saw, end to end, with no “and the rest is
obvious.” That is a hygiene fact about the gap-catcher, not a verdict on the argument:
it says I didn’t let myself skip a step where the machine was watching. It does not say
the statements are the right statements. That part stays on me, which is what the rest
of this section is about.

This is what it does NOT mean:

   • It does not mean the theorems prove physical reality. The theorems prove things
     about the Thiele substrate, as formalised in Coq. Whether that formalisation
     describes the world you live in is a separate question, answered by experiments,
     not by proof checkers.
   • It does not mean the Coq axioms themselves are consistent. Coq sits on a formal
     foundation called the Calculus of Inductive Constructions. The key idea is the
     Curry-Howard correspondence: proofs and programs are the same thing. A
     proof that P is true is literally a program of type P. Checking the proof is type-
     checking the program. The “inductive” part means you can define your own
     recursive types (naturals, lists, trees) inside the system instead of having them
     as magical primitives handed down from above. That matters. The foundation
     is explicit. Nothing is smuggled.
     Now the honest limitation. The sources I read on Coq’s foundation say it is
     believed to be logically consistent (meaning you cannot derive a contradiction
     from it), but this has not been proven within Coq itself. The way I understand it,
     that is not a weakness specific to Coq. The label I dug up is Gödel’s incomplete-
     ness theorem from 1931: a consistent, effectively axiomatized theory containing
     sufficient arithmetic cannot prove its own standard arithmetized consistency
     statement under the usual derivability conditions. And an inconsistent system
     proves everything, its own consistency included. So Coq’s foundation is trusted,
     not proved-from-within. “Trusted” has substance here. The same foundation
     has verified the CompCert C compiler and the Four Color Theorem (Gonthier
     2005, a landmark fully machine-checked proof of a major mathematical theorem,
     one first settled on paper-plus-computer in 1976), among other artifacts. That
     prior use is evidence about the foundation’s track record in practice. It is not a
     proof of logical consistency, and a successful large-scale application would not
     necessarily surface every possible inconsistency; an inconsistent system only
     breaks visibly if some proof actually exploits the inconsistency, and nothing
     here rules that out. I am relaying what I read. I have not personally audited
     CompCert or Gonthier’s proof. “Zero Admitted” means every theorem follows
     from those axioms. It does not mean those axioms are themselves in-system
     guaranteed. It means they are the same axioms a substantial body of verified


                                         167
The Thiele Machine                                           Devon Thiele • September 2026


      software has relied on without breaking, which is track record, not a consistency
      proof.
   • Hardware correspondence is conditional on its model and representation
     contract.   driven_step_wf proves intermediate model equality under
     WFDrivenPrecondition; executable RTL transfer adds finite-range and re-
     tirement obligations and toolchain trust. The four Q1+AB instructions are outside
     the synthesized inventory.
   • It does not mean the OCaml runner binary is proven inside Coq to be bitwise
     identical to the Coq semantics. The kernel proves Coq-side observable facts. The
     parity suite checks the extracted binary empirically. One is a theorem. The other
     is a test.

A theorem Coq checks proves its whole type, premises and record obligations in-
cluded. A Section hypothesis can turn into a theorem argument once the Section
closes. Print Assumptions lists global dependencies, and Closed under the
global context doesn’t make those arguments disappear. So to judge whether
a theorem covers the case you care about, read its statement, unfold the definitions
that carry the weight, check the global assumption report, and find witnesses for the
premises.

The bridges bring their own premises and their own trust boundaries. Any physical
reading needs its named quantum or Landauer–Unruh calibration premise. Getting
to running hardware also leans on extraction, the OCaml pretty-printer, BSC, the
generated RTL, and the simulation harness, and silicon claims add synthesis and
place-and-route on top. The Coq model theorem doesn’t verify that whole chain. The
OCaml and RTL parity checks are tests, each tied to its own revision and its own
inputs.

23 How to break this
Every main claim in this document comes with the exact shape of the thing that would
kill it. I went to the trouble on purpose. A claim you can’t break is a claim that wasn’t
saying anything, and I would rather hand you a loaded gun pointed at my own work
than a sentence too vague to aim at. So the ones below are written as instructions,
not as boasts: here is the trace to find, here is the object to build, here is the theorem
to exhibit. Build one and the proof tree falls, and I mean that as the better outcome
of the two, because a break tells me something I didn’t know and a year of nobody
finding one only tells me people got tired of looking. If you build any of these, that is
the message I want in my inbox.

Falsify No Free Insight.
Find a VM trace that:




                                           168
The Thiele Machine                                           Devon Thiele • September 2026


   • starts with vm_certified = false and vm_mu = 0
   • ends with vm_certified = true
   • ends with vm_mu = 0

If you find such a trace, the claim is false: certification_requires_positive_
mu breaks at the step that prices the cert-flip. Add the counterexample as a theorem
and the check refuses to compile on top of that.

Falsify µ-monotonicity.
Find a state s and instruction i such that (vm_apply s i ).µ < s.µ. The Coq proof of
vm_apply_mu falls.

Falsify categorical separation.
Show that the two witness states in categorical_separation differ in one of the
six computational fields (registers, memory, µ, PC, error, certification), or that their
morphism lists are the same. Either kills it. Error behavior is not the target; the thing
that separates them is the morphism graph itself.

Falsify Turing strictness.
Exhibit a classical program, a VM instruction trace restricted to the classical opcode
fragment (is_classical_program, run on the same substrate), whose execution
changes the partition graph (pg_next_id) or the morphism map. Or prove that the
witness step PNEW leaves pg_next_id fixed. Either kills the strictness half of D5_thi
ele_strictly_extends_classical. This is the semantic claim, that the classical
opcode fragment cannot reach the structural states one structural opcode reaches. It
is not the computability claim. A Turing machine can of course simulate a Thiele
run by encoding the graph as bytes on its tape; that simulation exists and is not a
counterexample.

Falsify structural entitlement.
Prove that for every pair of Thiele VM states with the same classical shadow and
different morphism structure, no probe preserves the distinction; equivalently, refute
the concrete witness pair the theorem exhibits. shadow_strictly_lossy falls. Or
produce a strict feasible-set subset with a distinguishing observation that does not
yield stronger membership predicates. The feasible-subset theorem falls with it.

Falsify universal certification cost.
Build a CertificationSystem satisfying the one-step rule cs_cert_costs, then
give a trace from uncertified to certified with total cost 0. universal_nfi_any_sub
strate falls. If you instead drop the one-step rule, you have found a free-certification
system, not a counterexample.




                                          169
The Thiele Machine                                             Devon Thiele • September 2026


Falsify substrate-level structural-axis undecidability.
Three routes (Section 10 expands).

   • Exhibit a Coq function decide : Program → bool whose diagonal flip
     transformer is internally representable in some Substrate plus WithShortcut-
     Predicate instance and that satisfies the bi-implication. The substrate-level proof
     of structural_shortcut_undecidable must fail at some step; identify
     which.
   • For the nat-coded substrate specifically: exhibit a Coq function d : nat →
     bool that correctly decides nat_admits d. The corollary nat_self_undec
     idable says no such d exists; build one and you have contradicted it head-on,
     which is exactly the kind of break I want to hear about.
   • For the VM specialization, supply its bounded recurrence premise for the cho-
     sen representability class and a correct decider whose particular diagonal flip
     belongs to that class. A correct external decider alone is not a counterexample:
     bounded membership is externally decidable, and its flip is excluded by every
     class satisfying that recurrence premise.


Falsify the classical CHSH bound.
Exhibit four fixed bits a0 , a1 , b0 , b1 and a tally consistent with that one strategy
(WCLocallyConsistent, every setting pair sampled) whose score beats 2. chsh
_stat_violation_not_local falls. So does known mathematics. A short run
from a randomized local strategy that lands past 2 by luck does not count; the theorem
is about one fixed deterministic strategy, and Section 18’s coin-flip example already
shows that luck.

Falsify the CHSH-NPA bridge.
The biconditional column_contractive_iff_npa_psd falls to a counterex-
ample in either direction: a column-contractive correlator whose specified zero-
marginal, zero-within-party-moment matrix is not PSD (forward), or a PSD specified
zero-marginal, zero-within-party-moment matrix whose correlators are not column-
contractive (reverse). For the Tsirelson corollary, exhibit a slice-coherent (column-
                                    √
contractive) correlator with |S| > 2 2 and state_column_contractive_impl
ies_tsirelson falls. The PR box and the Turing-classical point (1, 0, 1, 0) are not
counterexamples: both fail column contractivity by direct arithmetic, which is what
the theorem requires, not what would break it.

Falsify the elliptope completion.
Three targets, one per load-bearing theorem of Section 18.8. Exhibit signs a0 , a1 , b0 , b1
with squares 1 and a vector on which the completed Gram form goes negative; dete
rministic_strategy_elliptope falls. Exhibit an elliptope-realizable correlator
tuple with S2 > 8; elliptope_tsirelson falls, and with it the claim that the


                                            170
The Thiele Machine                                          Devon Thiele • September 2026


completion cannot stretch both Cauchy–Schwarz budgets at once. Exhibit real x, y
making the PR box’s completed 5 × 5 matrix PSD; pr_box_not_elliptope falls.
The µ = 0 all-ones witness and (1, 0, 1, 0) are not counterexamples here either, in the
opposite direction from the bridge entry above: the elliptope theorems admit them,
which is what the theorems require.

Falsify the elliptope gate.
elliptope_check_full_sound claims soundness with the check’s passing as its
only hypothesis: any passing combination of witness counters, completion buckets,
or LDL certificate entails elliptope membership of the witness-derived correlators. Ex-
hibit inputs where elliptope_check_full returns true while the correlators lie
outside the set (violating S2 ≤ 8 suffices), and the soundness theorem falls, taking el
liptope_full_gate_never_accepts_pr_box with it. Trap-side behavior is not
a target, and a rejection is never a counterexample to soundness. This gate’s certificate
format requires rational completion or LDL data (Section 18.8), so by construction it
cannot accept a boundary point whose every PSD completion is irrational. That’s a
fact about this particular certificate format. Nothing here claims every exact integer
check has to reject exactly those points and no others, and nothing needs that.

The Inquisitor standard.
Run the Inquisitor on the repository and read what it says. It fails on HIGH or
MEDIUM findings. LOW findings get reported but don’t fail it on their own. Ev-
ery in-source scope marker sits next to the code it describes, with its reason. The
Inquisitor reads syntax and applies heuristics. It can’t tell you whether a theorem
applies to your case, and it doesn’t read the prose around it. A new finding means
somebody looks. It doesn’t automatically mean a theorem is false.

Falsify the axiom audit.
If running Print Assumptions (Section A: the Coq command that reports global
proof dependencies) on any named theorem in the kernel tier shows a project-local
Axiom or Parameter in the dependency tree, the audit has degraded. The full-
corpus probe covers 14,402 named theorems across 504 files: 6,855 close under
the global Coq context outright, and 7,547 use only Coq standard-library assump-
tions. The receipt names five such constants: dependent functional extensionality,
Eqdep.Eq_rect_eq.eq_rect_eq, ClassicalDedekindReals.sig_not_dec,
ClassicalDedekindReals.sig_forall_dec,                                            and
Classical_Prop.classic. Zero project-local axioms appear in any of the
14,402 dependency trees. This audits where global dependencies come from. It
doesn’t prove that a theorem’s own Section-generalized premises hold for the instance
you care about; those premises stay visible in the theorem types and get checked
separately. Any project-local axiom or parameter appearing in a global dependency
tree is a bug.



                                          171
The Thiele Machine                                          Devon Thiele • September 2026


Falsify the hardware bisimulation.
A counterexample to driven_step_wf has to satisfy WFDrivenPrecondition and
break its stated intermediate model equality. A cosimulation mismatch on a valid
input challenges the tested RTL transfer for that revision. Then pull it apart piece by
piece: encoding, when the observation was taken, extraction, pretty-printing, BSC,
and the harness. An input outside the theorem’s domain, or a toolchain mismatch,
doesn’t refute the conditional Coq theorem.

Falsify the convergence answer to parametricity.
The separation/irredundance/uniqueness trio is schema-parametric: any field a step
rule carries that classical state does not determine supports all three theorems, word
for word, and I concede that in full. What I offer for certification is convergence, and
it is weaker than a proof. Three results from vocabularies that owe each other nothing
each turn on a priced commitment: the verifier factorisation result (V_does_not_f
actor_through_classical) is about a claim on the ledger, the clean-room floor
demonstration (minimal/nofi_demo.py) is about narrowing a set of possibilities,
and the NPA moment-matrix certificate (chsh_lassert_no_trap_implies_npa
_psd) is a priced check on counters. They do not land on the same state field. The
CHSH check never writes the certification flag, and the verifier result is about µ. What
they share is the shape: something gets committed, and the commitment is where the
cost lands. Exhibit another event with three independent bridges of the same standing,
or break one of these, and certification’s distinguished seat falls back to an aesthetic
choice. Certification as the event a model is forced to price is conjectural. Section 26
states the redundant-record criterion and its attack surface; the two-observer toggle
game refutes the implication from consensus, exact observation, and coordinator-free
evolution to permanence.

Falsify the proof-of-stake reduction.
Construct a trace in the formal finality model satisfying the stated slashing schedule,
with unfinalized initial state, finalized final state, and zero summed modeled charge.
This contradicts slashing_finality_floor. A deployment with different transi-
tion or accounting semantics instead challenges its proposed interpretation.

Falsify the gas-metering reduction.
Construct a schedule satisfying both the commitment floor and no-overcharge whose
charging predicate differs from the commitment predicate on some reachable step. ga
s_schedule_exactness falls, and the kernel’s exact_commitment_pricing_c
haracterization with it.

Falsify the attestation reduction.
Build a sound and complete attestation verifier for the µ-dependent claim that reads
only the bare classical transcript, a function of the report’s log field alone. attes
tation_cannot_factor_through_bare_transcript falls, and behind it the


                                          172
The Thiele Machine                                            Devon Thiele • September 2026


verifier-factorisation theorem.

Falsify the transparency-log reduction.
Build a sound and complete log-free verifier with the same soundness target. abstra
ct_bare_verifier_impossible falls, and the bare-setting impossibility with it.

Falsify the permanent-certificate theorem.
Exhibit a finite, duplicate-free list of every state, a certificate no step revokes, and an
injective step that switches it on. permanent_flip_is_not_injective falls, and
a2_from_merging_price_and_permanence with it. An infinite state space, a
revocable certificate, or an unpriced merge is not a counterexample; the file already
builds one of each.

Falsify the cost of a squeeze and the merge-or-revoke dichotomy.
For the bound, exhibit a finite machine with a permanent certificate and a cost as-
signment meeting compression_priced where one instruction switches k states
on beside m certified ones with 2cost m < m + k. For the dichotomy, exhibit a finite
machine and an injective instruction that switches a reading on without switching it
off anywhere. permanent_flips_compression_bound or flip_merges_or_
revokes falls. A merging instruction that also revokes is not a counterexample to
anything; forced_price_without_permanent_record is one.

Falsify the shadow-pricing theorem.
Exhibit a window and a price computed from the observed before and after that meets
the floor and never overcharges on a machine where two steps share their before
and after pictures and only one certifies. shadow_cannot_price_exactly falls.
For the VM claim, show that CERTIFY 0 and JUMP 1 0 from the clean start differ
on bare_observable, or that CERTIFY 0 and JUMP 1 1 differ on forget. A
window that shows certification is not a counterexample; window_showing_readi
ng_prices_exactly says it prices exactly.

Falsify the entropy bound.
Exhibit a finite machine with a permanent certificate, an instruction that switches
k ≥ 1 states on beside m certified ones, and a distribution on those m + k states whose
image under the step has Shannon entropy above log2 m. permanent_step_entro
py_ceiling falls. A heat measurement below k B T ln((m + k )/m) does not falsify the
theorem. It falsifies the premise landauer_heat for that device, which is Landauer’s
principle. For the general version, exhibit a deterministic step and a distribution under
which two different states of positive probability land together while the entropy does
not drop; entropy_drop_pos_of_support_merge falls. A certifying step from a
known state that dissipates no heat is not a counterexample; known_state_flip_f
orces_no_heat says that case owes none.



                                           173
The Thiele Machine                                          Devon Thiele • September 2026


Falsify the finite VM instance.
Exhibit a VM state and one of CERTIFY 0, JUMP a 2, CHECKPOINT "" 0 whose
result, read as program counter modulo four and certification flag, differs from the
finite machine’s step, or whose µ rise differs from 1, 2, 0. vm_runs_finite_machi
ne or vm_pays_finite_price falls. A VM program that uses JUMP a 0 is not a
counterexample. It is outside the fragment, and the full VM leaves that merge free on
purpose (vm_jump_is_free_merge). To break the full-VM claim instead, exhibit a
VM step that switches the certification flag on without merging two states, or at zero
cost; vm_certifying_step_is_priced_merge falls.

Falsify the proof-carrying reduction.
Construct a level-k certified proof script with total µ below k, or a sound and complete
zero-round bare verifier. level_k_certification_cost_floor (restated for this
model as level_k_verification_floor, an alias whose proof is that theorem) or
bare_pcc_impossible falls, and the µ-hierarchy or the bare-setting impossibility
behind them.

23.1 Contribution and attribution boundary
This section says what is mine and what is not. It is not a claim to have been first. I
have not read widely enough to say any result below is historically new, and I am not
going to pretend otherwise. Two comparisons earlier in the document are primary:
Landauer’s erasure result and Wolpert and Macready’s No-Free-Lunch theorem. The
other labels are orientation points, and I would welcome correction on any of them.

The work comes apart into five layers.

Borrowed mathematics and vocabulary.
Category theory, Turing and Church–Turing terminology, the Kleene and Rice diag-
onal patterns, Bell, CHSH and Tsirelson, NPA and the elliptope, the thermodynamic
motivation, and the ordinary definitions of projections, partitions, predicates and re-
source measures all come from other people and from standard mathematics. So do
the facts under the permanent-certificate results. Setting a bit to one whatever it held
is Landauer’s own example of an irreversible step (1961). That finite memory eventu-
ally forces erasure is Bennett’s point about reversible computing. The counting is the
pigeonhole principle. Entropy is at most the logarithm of the support size, and a deter-
ministic step never raises it; both are Shannon’s. That Landauer’s bound depends on
the actual distribution, so a known state can be reset for free, is the generalized form
of Maroney (2009) and Sagawa (2014). None of it is mine.
Formal reconstructions.
The Coq development defines the state types, transition systems, fixed real-matrix
predicates, encodings and proof terms this monograph uses. When I say a theorem is
checked in Coq, I mean the stated proposition has a kernel-checked proof under its
printed premises. That does not make the underlying idea mine, and it does not make


                                          174
The Thiele Machine                                                 Devon Thiele • September 2026


it new.
Results this project proved.
Inside the stated interfaces, the repository proves the abstract A2 certification floor,
the fixed-schedule ledger identity and uniqueness, the named projection-separation
witnesses, the trace-fold universal property, the conditional structural-entitlement rep-
resentation theorem, and the substrate-diagonal results. It also proves the permanent-
certificate results of Section 9: that any implementation of a certificate no step revokes
must merge on the certifying step, the logarithmic bound on the squeeze, the merge-
or-revoke dichotomy and “forced to be priced exactly when merging”, the entropy
forms with their distribution boundary, that an exact price for certification can’t be
computed from a colliding window, the finite VM instance, and what the full VM
prices. The facts they rest on are the borrowed ones above. What is mine is putting
them together about certification, and checking every step in Coq. These are claims
about the explicit Coq contracts and witnesses. They are results of this project. I am
not claiming nobody wrote down an equivalent statement before me.
Instances and engineering.
The 51-opcode VM, its cost schedule, the Python and extracted-OCaml runners, the
Kami model, the Bluespec and Verilog path, the verification scripts, the finite tests,
and the synthesis and FPGA workflow are things I built. How much of each is proved,
tested, or trusted is the edge-by-edge account in Section 16 and docs/ASSURANCE.md.
A tested edge stays a tested edge. I do not promote it to a theorem.
Conjectures and excluded claims.
The corpus does not prove a unique structural core, a physical meaning for the ledger,
a universal class of priced events beyond the stated merge result, unconditional full-
ISA hardware equality, or broad quantum and complexity-class conclusions. A condi-
tional contract does not imply any of them.

So the claim ledger that follows is a map from formal names to their domains and
premises. It is not a list of discoveries.

24 The full claim ledger
This is the lookup list: every main claim by its Coq name, each checked under its
complete formal premises. The crosswalk after it records where each one applies and
where its instances stop. For the full quantified statement, go to the named source
declaration.

  12. certification_is_lost: certification can be in the kernel of the classical forgetful map.
  13. shadow_strictly_lossy: the classical shadow forgets morphism structure.
  14. mu_ledger_necessity: no strict-classical oracle on (mem, regs, pc) recovers the pair

      instruction-cost schedule.

      The cost is not just δ, it is δ for ordinary instructions, S(δ) for cert-setters,
      |payload|bits + S(δ) for EMIT, etc.
  21. kernel_certified_implies_positive_mu: from zero, reaching certified implies µ > 0.
  22. feasible_strict_subset_implies_strict_predicates: feasible-set narrowing plus a

      nat-weighted feasible sets.
  29. structural_entitlement_representation: strict narrowing plus distinguishability, certified
      posterior admissibility, and an explicit posterior-representative reduction imply a

  30. every_sound_structural_shortcut_lands_here: every packaged SoundStructuralShort

  31. structural_shortcut_undecidable: for every Substrate plus WithShortcutPredicate instance,

                            

      orthogonal-observables slice of the quantum elliptope (ρ AA = ρ BB = 0), not the full
      elliptope. Classical correlations like (1, 0, 1, 0) have |S| ≤ 2 but fail column contractivity;
      the gate is a slice-membership test, not a truth test, and it rejects all sixteen
      deterministic local strategies. The classical polytope and the slice-coherent set intersect
      but neither contains the other.
