      lliptope_check_full_sound (in ElliptopeGate.v) adds the decidable
      two-branch Z-arithmetic gate (fraction-free Sylvester on completion buckets, rational
      LDLT certificate reaching singular/boundary completions): a passing check entails
      membership, with computed acceptances (gate_accepts_all_ones, gate_accep
      ts_turing_point, gate_accepts_beyond_classical, and on-boundary gate_
      accepts_pythagorean_boundary) and the universal refusal elliptope_full_g
      ate_never_accepts_pr_box. Correlator level only; the runtime opcode is
      unchanged. This one-way soundness theorem does not establish completeness for the
      full set of quantum behaviors.
  61. mu_hierarchy_theorem: for every k ≥ 1, there exists a level-k certified trace with cost
      exactly k, and every trace that ends certified having executed a CERTIFY at level k
      (charge S(δ) ≥ k) pays total cost ≥ k. Unbounded strict µ-cost levels: no finite budget
      caps every certification. Not a problem-class separation (Section 25).
  62. mu_hierarchy_no_upper_bound: no fixed µ-budget suffices for all levels.
  63. Structural-invariant Qed group: the 10 opcodes PNEW, PSPLIT, PMERGE, MORPH,
      MORPH_ID, MORPH_DELETE, MORPH_ASSERT, MORPH_GET, COMPOSE,
      MORPH_TENSOR carry Qed bisimulation proofs under the inductive invariant
      coupling_wf (= coupling_desc_bounded ∧ coupling_pairs_in_range ∧
      coupling_pairs_fully_populated) and morph_table_wf. The preservation
      theorems coupling_wf_kami_step_preserved (under coupling_desc_safe,
      itself preserved) and morph_table_wf_kami_step_preserved show these
      invariant components survive every kami_step. The instruction-specific premises
      (nonempty region, distinct merge operands, table capacity, module bounds) stay in
      WFDrivenPrecondition and are checked per step by WFDrivenRun; the invariants
      do not discharge them.
  64. RTL translation boundary: the Section Variable rtl_step_correct is a contract for the



                                              179
The Thiele Machine                                                  Devon Thiele • September 2026


      synthesized-RTL relationship, not a theorem that the generated Verilog automatically
      satisfies. The Coq theorem driven_step_wf covers the intermediate Kami model
      under WFDrivenPrecondition; the emitted RTL and later FPGA artifacts are
      checked by the cosimulation, pipeline-manifest, synthesis, place-and-route, and
      bitstream gates for their recorded source/tool revisions. bsc_kami_compilation_t
      rusted names the BSC/compiler and printer trust boundary. No unconditional
      end-to-end RTL/bitstream bisimulation is claimed.
  65. nothing_at_stake_is_free_forgery: the specific nothing_at_stake_gadget, whose
      finalize step carries zero stake-at-risk, admits no A2 field. Nothing-at-stake is the
      kernel’s free forgery, stated in proof-of-stake vocabulary (Section 17).
  66. slashing_finality_floor: for the slashing gadget built from a given stake-at-risk function
      that charges PoSFinalize at least 1 (any such function), every run from unfinalized
      to finalized carries total stake-at-risk ≥ 1; universal_nfi_any_substrate
      instantiated at the slashing gadget.
  67. gas_schedule_exactness: a gas schedule satisfies the commitment floor and no-overcharge
      exactly when its charging predicate is the commitment predicate with unit pricing; the
      kernel machine itself inhabits the class (thiele_vm_commit_pricing_is_exact).
  68. attestation_cannot_factor_through_bare_transcript: a sound and complete attestation
      verifier of the µ-dependent claim cannot factor through the report’s bare transcript; the
      replay attack is the witness pair (replay_is_a_two_preimage_witness).
  69. level_k_verification_floor: a VM run from the clean start that ends certified after executing
      a CERTIFY charged at least k pays at least k µ (an alias of level_k_certification_
      cost_floor), with tightness witnessed; bounds certification events only, not gate
      counts, circuit size, prover time, or proof length.
  70. permanent_flip_is_not_injective: on a finite state space with a certificate that no step
      revokes, any step that switches the certificate on is not injective.
  71. a2_from_merging_price_and_permanence / permanent_certification_trace_floor: under the
      same finiteness and permanence, pricing every non-injective step at ≥ 1 yields A2, and
      the trace-level floor follows through universal_nfi_any_substrate.
  72. permanent_flips_collapse_at_least: a step that switches on k distinct uncertified states has
      at least k fewer distinct images than inputs.
  73. honest_erasure_accounting_implies_a2 / commit_without_erasure_system_is_not_honest:
      erasure accounting that reports every merge charges a permanent certification flip on a
      finite machine at least 1; the zero-cost erasure rival leaves its own merge unreported.
  74. permanent_flips_compression_bound / permanent_flips_log_bound: under the counting
      premise that each unit of cost pays for one halving, switching k states on when m are
      certified costs enough that m + k ≤ 2cost m, so log2↑ (m + k ) ≤ cost + log2↑ m; quad_stam
      p_needs_two and quad_stamp_cost_two_is_priced show the bound is
      attained.
  75. forced_priced_iff_merges: an instruction is charged by every merge-pricing cost
      assignment exactly when its step merges two states.
  76. flip_merges_or_revokes / injective_flip_revokes: on a finite machine, an instruction that
      switches a reading on either merges two states or switches the reading off somewhere
      else.



                                               180
The Thiele Machine                                                 Devon Thiele • September 2026


  77. permanent_record_write_is_forced_priced / forced_price_without_permanent_record:
      permanent-record writes are forced to be priced; the converse fails on a three-state
      machine.
  78. shadow_cannot_price_exactly / window_showing_reading_prices_exactly: if two steps share
      their observed before and after and only one certifies, no price computed from the
      window meets the floor and never overcharges; a window that shows the reading
      prices exactly.
  79. vm_bare_shadow_cannot_price_exactly / vm_forget_shadow_cannot_price_exactly: the VM
      has such a pair for bare_observable and for forget, from the clean start.
  80. permanent_step_entropy_ceiling / permanent_flip_uniform_entropy_drop: after a permanent
      step, a distribution on the m certified states and the k flipping ones has Shannon
      entropy at most log2 m; from the uniform distribution the step removes at least
      log2 ((m + k)/m) bits.
  81. a2_from_entropy_price_and_permanence / entropy_priced_trace_floor: a whole-unit price
      that covers the bits each instruction removes satisfies A2 on a finite machine with a
      permanent certificate, with the trace floor through universal_nfi_any_substrat
      e.
  82. permanent_flip_heat_floor / permanent_flip_heat_positive: under the named premise landa
      uer_heat, from the uniform distribution on the m certified and k flipping states, that
      flip dissipates at least k B T ln((m + k)/m), which is positive when k ≥ 1 and T > 0.
  83. entropy_drop_as_point_sum / entropy_drop_pos_of_support_merge: for any distribution, the
      entropy a deterministic step removes is a sum of nonnegative per-state terms, positive
      when two states of positive probability land together.
  84. permanent_flip_full_support_heat_positive / known_state_flip_forces_no_heat: a permanent
      flip forces positive heat under landauer_heat at positive temperature for every
      distribution positive on the states in play; from a known state it removes no entropy
      and forces none.
  85. fin_merging_priced / fin_compression_priced: the eight-state machine with FCertify,
      FJump, FNext at prices 1, 2, 0 is finite, permanent, and meets both pricing premises.
  86. vm_runs_finite_trace / vm_fragment_certification_paid: read through program counter
      modulo four and the certification flag, CERTIFY 0, JUMP a 2, CHECKPOINT "" 0
      run that machine, µ rises by its price, and a run of them that certifies raises µ by at least
      one.
  87. vm_certifying_step_is_priced_merge / vm_prices_certifying_merge_leaves_others_free: on the
      full VM, every step that switches the certification flag on merges two states and costs at
      least one, while JUMP 0 0 merges program counters at cost zero, so the full VM prices
      the certifying merge and not every merge.


Bridge-tier claims (checked in Coq, but resting on named hypotheses or documented
trust boundaries):

   • Hardware correspondence: driven_step_wf and driven_trace_commu
     tes prove intermediate model equality under WFDrivenPrecondition and
     WFDrivenRun. Section 16 states the additional finite RTL contract and distin-


                                               181
The Thiele Machine                                            Devon Thiele • September 2026


      guishes the 47 synthesized instructions from four nonsynthesized checks. As-
      sessing an executable instance also requires allocation, overflow, and retirement
      obligations.
   • OCaml extraction: ocaml_observable_nofi_and_monotone proves NoFI
     and µ-monotonicity for the Coq-side extracted observable. The external OCaml
     binary equivalence is a parity-test trust boundary, not a kernel theorem.
   • Structural advantage: the kernel proves exact µ costs and the arithmetic time-tax
     facts for the factored-search witness; executable tests check the measured VM
     behavior.
   • LASSERT cost formula: lassert_cost_from_component_floors and
     lassert_cost_is_its_formula. A cost meeting the state-term and
     description-term premises is at least the formula. That is addition over premises.
     Merge pricing does not force the state term (observer_narrowing_can_be_
     free), and a physical reading also needs Landauer-Unruh calibration.

Part IV

Boundary of the proved results

25 Scope of the claim: what is established, what is not
This is the line around what I have proved. The named projections lose distinctions
that matter to the richer machine. A2 gives an accounting law, and the cost and initial-
ity results have the contracts stated earlier. Claims beyond that line are conjectures or
outside the formal model.

Physical interpretation of µ. The ledger is unique once the starting value and the
schedule are chosen. That doesn’t make it entropy, and it doesn’t make it energy. A
physical bridge needs a model of which states carry information, premises that can
all hold at once, and a real argument tying the formal charge to dissipation. The full-
ISA F1 premise pair fails the very first test: its universal collapse premise contradicts
free jumps. The permanent-certificate theorem (Section 9.1) passes the first two. Its
premises hold together, and finite memory accounts for retained history. Its entropy
version (Section 9.5) states the price in bits, and turns bits into joules only through
Landauer’s principle as a named premise. Tying those units to measured heat is the


                                           186
The Thiele Machine                                            Devon Thiele • September 2026


part no proof here can do.

Class separation. The witnesses show specific windows losing specific structural
fields. They don’t stop a conventional simulator from keeping those fields in its
encoding. No separation of standard complexity classes follows from these collisions,
or from the charge levels climbing without bound.

Program predicates and state observations. The state-level CSR collision and the
program-level diagonal live in different domains. Linking them would take a theorem
of its own. The nat family and the bounded VM theorem keep their explicit quantifiers
and representability premises.

Feasible-set cost bounds. The representation theorem is handed its tree and its repre-
sentative reduction, along with the covering and payment conditions, the uncertified
start, and the certified end. It doesn’t dig the tree out of an arbitrary deterministic run,
and it isn’t a Shannon bound on every trace.

Full quantum behavior. The fixed-matrix biconditional is about one specified com-
pletion, with zero marginals and zero within-party moments. The elliptope lets the
within-party moments move, at the level of correlators. Full behaviors with marginals,
and actual operator realizations, need more identifications than that. A sound runtime
check doesn’t make it complete for those bigger sets.

Those are the edges of the individual results. Every structural, conditional, and con-
sistency result keeps its hypotheses attached. What I’m proposing reaches further
than certification, but no theorem here puts an unavoidable physical price on every
observable change.

Trace initiality and foundational interpretation. The universal property is about eval-
uating instruction lists (Section 3.7). Ledger uniqueness assumes a schedule. Exact
pricing assumes an event, and a reference count that says what overcharging means.
Those theorems make the construction precise. They don’t show that its chosen cate-
gory, or its chosen event, is physically necessary.

The broader research question. Certification is one worked example. A family of
observations might turn up other useful structural laws, but each would need its
events, its evidence, and its cost rules justified on their own. The separation and
oracle results are about what specific maps keep. They don’t show infinitely many
independent dimensions, zero mutual information, or a cost for changing coordinates.

Where each result lives. Some results hold for every machine that carries a record the
way this book asks. Others hold for the machine I built. The split matters, because the
structural result is one record axis over a free base.

   • Every system that follows A2, whatever its states and instructions: the certifica-
     tion floor (universal_nfi_any_substrate), at most as many certifications


                                            187
The Thiele Machine                                         Devon Thiele • September 2026


      as total cost (flips_le_cost), and A2 as a lower-bound potential argument
      (a2_iff_nonnegative_amortized_cost).
   • Every finite machine whose price covers merges: A2 for records that never
     switch off (a2_from_merging_price_and_permanence), and which in-
     structions every such price must charge (forced_priced_iff_merges).
   • Every finite machine whose price pays one unit per halving (compression_pr
     iced): the machine’s own narrowing (run_narrowing_priced_log).
   • Every machine that runs a base, with a record driven by its computation that
     never switches off: the latch form (record_axis_is_latch_holds).
   • Every window through which a certifying step and a non-certifying one look
     alike: no price read off the window is exact (shadow_cannot_price_exactl
     y).
   • The Thiele build only: the VM’s cost schedule and its numbers, the collisions of
     the VM’s own windows, the CHSH gate and its NPA bridge, the Kami hardware
     correspondence, the 51-opcode instruction set, and the finite VM instance.

Extensions and their present boundary.

   • A finite monotone multi-valued record decomposes into its threshold latches.
     The stronger claim that it is one Boolean latch is false.
   • Actual revocation, including revocation priced from the other side as in Casper,
     lies outside the permanent-record axis. A revocation price alone does not transfer
     to a price on writing the record.
   • The cross-base comparison satisfies the elementary equivalence laws, which
     ignore instruction granularity by allowing stuttering. The Turing machine, a
     Cook–Reckhow RAM, L, and the VM each have a closed executable base adapter
     (record_axis_is_latch_on_tm_holds, record_axis_is_latch_on_
     ram_holds, record_axis_is_latch_on_l_holds, record_axis_is_l
     atch_on_vm_holds). The latch form holds on every base, so it does not tell
     them apart.
   • Permanence forces logical non-injectivity on finite state spaces. It does not by
     itself prove that every economic, cryptographic, or physical realization pays one
     universal kind of cost (Section 17.7).
   • The two-state calorimeter protocol fixes microscopic populations, a Hamilto-
     nian, a discrete master equation, and exact bath heat. It also proves that the
     same population dynamics admit different heat when the energy gap changes.
     Landauer’s value follows only after selecting the corresponding gap; calibration
     to a thermally admissible device requires empirical evidence.
   • A direct probabilistic analogue of uniqueness up to schedule is false: two ma-
     chines can agree on their base distribution and disagree on the record distribu-

                                         188
The Thiele Machine                                          Devon Thiele • September 2026


      tion.
   • The guest VM supports the concrete Rice reduction of Section 10, numeric de-
     coding, verified fuel-bounded dispatch, semantic s-m-n specialization, and its
     own recursion theorem (vm_guest_recursion_theorem_closed): every
     program transformer that a guest program computes on program codes has a
     fixed point that matches it in all four final registers and in µ on every input,
     halting or not. The evaluator inside that proof runs as guest code: it is extracted
     to the lambda calculus L, compiled to a Minsky machine, and executed by the
     guest.

26 The pointer-observable conjecture
Why put an event on the meter? The substitution contest fixes the event and compares
its chargers. I want to go after the choice itself. My candidate is record proliferation:
a distinction matters across an ecosystem when separate observers keep enough ev-
idence to recover it. That gives me a criterion to test, and something specific to be
wrong about.

Here is the criterion, stated as a conjecture and nothing more.

      Conjecture (the pointer-observable criterion). Among the events a step
      rule could meter, certification is singled out by redundant record prolifer-
      ation: a certification event is the kind of event whose record gets copied,
      checked, and stored across independent substrates that do not otherwise
      share state, while rival meterable events leave no such redundant trail.
      The event a substrate discipline is forced to price is the one whose records
      proliferate.

The name is borrowed deliberately. In the decoherence literature, a pointer observable
is the one the environment keeps redundant records of: many independent fragments
of the world each carry enough information to reconstruct the pointer value, and that
redundancy, not any intrinsic label, is what makes those observables the objective,
classical-looking ones. I am conjecturing that certification stands to computation the
way pointer observables stand to quantum states: not privileged by fiat, privileged by
being the thing everything else keeps copies of. Whether that analogy is load-bearing
or just load-shaped is exactly what the conjecture is for; I am naming it, not claiming
it.

Section 17 gives the idea five concrete settings: proof-of-stake finality, gas metering,
trusted-execution attestation, certificate transparency, and proof-carrying verification.
These disciplines supply the motivation. I supply the formal models, including what
each observer sees and which events compete for the meter. That choice is part of the
argument. The proofs cannot make it for me. A public certificate may be checkable
without every observer keeping a copy; a paid instruction may have no independently
stored record. The criterion has to face those cases.

                                          189
The Thiele Machine                                           Devon Thiele • September 2026


The permanent-certificate theorem (Section 9.1) gives the question a second handle.
That theorem never uses the word certification for anything special. It prices any
yes/no reading that, once on, stays on, on a machine with finite memory. So it points
at a narrower candidate: the events to price are the ones that write a record no later
step can take back. Certification is one of them. I tested that candidate in Section 9.3.
On finite hardware, the events every merge-pricing account must price are the merges,
every permanent-record write is a merge, and the only free flips are ones the same
step can revoke. The candidate as an exact answer is false: a merging step can also
revoke. Whether the records that proliferate are the permanent ones, and whether
proliferation picks out anything the merge criterion doesn’t, is what the two handles
have to settle between them.

How to attack it, because a conjecture without a target painted on it is just a mood.
Exhibit a deployed, independently evolved metering discipline that prices a non-
certification event with the same conviction these five price certification, an event
whose records do not proliferate, and the criterion is dead. Or show the criterion fails
to discriminate: that multiplication counts or head-reversals proliferate redundantly
in some substrate ecosystem by the same standard I just applied, so the criterion picks
out more than one event and decides nothing. Or formalize the criterion and show
certification fails it. Any of the three would teach me more than another instantiation
would.

PointerObservable.v defines the objects: an Ecosystem of observers each hold-
ing a boolean fragment of the state; records, one observer’s fragment deciding an
event; redundantly_proliferating, every observer recording it; and unique_
pointer_among, proliferating while no named rival event does, with uniqueness
deliberately relative to the rival family because absolute uniqueness is false for free
(an event’s complement proliferates whenever it does). The record has no predicate
saying the observers are independent; that stays part of the modeling claim. The
conjecture itself is stated as a schema (pointer_criterion_holds): over any class
of ecosystems with designated metered events and rivals, every metered event is the
unique pointer among its rivals.

PointerObservableReductions.v supplies five minimal observer models and
proves five_labeled_models_have_selected_pointer. The choice of observ-
able fragments, metered events, and rival families is explicit. All five are one construc-
tion with the fields renamed. These are five formal instances, not five independent
empirical validations of the criterion.

The five proofs establish the criterion inside those five models. To use them as an
argument about deployed systems, I owe the correspondence: why those fragments
describe the observers, why those rivals are the relevant rivals, and why their records
should govern the price. Five proofs about chosen models do not settle those choices.
That is where the conjecture has to earn its reach.


                                           190
The Thiele Machine                                          Devon Thiele • September 2026


A second survey fixes twelve candidate events across the proof-of-stake, gas, attes-
tation, transparency, proof-carrying, MAC, and signature models. The Coq bundle
checks the twelve declared observer-map classifications and a swapped variant in
which the criterion selects the newly proliferating event. One candidate labelled MAC
authenticity is non-proliferating in that map. The security relevance of the label and
the faithfulness of the observer choice are prose premises, so this is a model counterex-
ample, not a theorem about every deployed MAC system.

A stronger necessity theorem also fails before any cryptography enters. Ecosyst
emGame.v gives two observers whose views exactly equal the event, who always
agree, and who update by the same local rule with no observer index or coordinator
state. The shared rule is Boolean negation. Starting from true, both observers and
the event become false, so toggle_game_refutes_strong_pointer_necess
ity proves that consensus, sound and complete observation, and coordinator-free
evolution do not force a permanent commit. If durable views are added, permanence
follows (durable_consensus_implies_permanence), but persistence is then the
load-bearing premise. On the VM, vm_certification_is_permanent_consen
sus instantiates that durable case: observers read vm_certified, and each step is
one fixed instruction. This rules out the proposed route from distributed agreement
alone to the pointer criterion; it does not rule out a narrower theorem with substantive
network, adversary, or durability assumptions.

Counterexamples are part of the argument. PointerObservableCounterexampl
es.v runs an adversarial search against the strong reading of the criterion: deniable
authentication, symmetric authentication, and object capabilities. That each design
resists forgery and is independently deployed is argued in prose. The Coq half fixes
an observer map for each and checks that the event fails to proliferate in it. It does
not prove cryptographic security or establish deployment facts. On the argued cases,
a rule claiming that forgery resistance alone forces every observer to retain evidence
fails.

Being publicly checkable and actually getting copied everywhere are different things.
A way to verify something existing doesn’t mean every observer keeps a record of
it. The abstract verifier examples give three ways to add evidence, each with its own
guarantee. They don’t show those are the only ways, or that only one of them can
involve metering.

So the real question is whether a well-argued choice of observers and rival events
picks out metering disciplines worth having. V_does_not_factor_through_cla
ssical proves that a sound and complete verifier for the distinguishing claim can’t
get by on the colliding bare observation alone. It doesn’t pick one physical way to
supply the missing evidence.

The conjecture asks which events deserve a cost law. The accounting theorems tell me
what follows once that law is specified. I want both parts of the argument on the table,


                                          191
The Thiele Machine                                          Devon Thiele • September 2026


with neither borrowing a proof from the other.

27 Conclusion
The Thiele Machine is an abstract model of computation, and this book has been an
argument made with it. The argument is that the usual account of a computation,
what it reads and writes, is a shadow, and that the axis it leaves out belongs in the
step. That axis holds what a computation established and what establishing it cost.

Here is where each step of the argument ended up. The shadow loses something real:
proved, for every window I named, with witness pairs you can print. Establishing is
not free: proved, for any system that follows A2, whatever its states and instructions.
And on finite hardware with certificates that last, A2 itself follows from Landauer’s
principle: proved, with that principle as the named premise, in counted states and in
bits. A finite piece of the VM is such a machine, and the VM runs it at exactly that
price. In the full VM, every certification flip is a priced merge and JUMP 0 0 is a free
merge, so the VM does not price every merge. Leaning on a fact takes a paid history:
defined, with the cost floor proved for shortcuts that hand over their receipts. The
axis belongs in the step: that is the proposal. Two parts are proved. An account that
prices certification exactly can’t compute its price from the window, because the VM
has a certifying step and a non-certifying one that look the same through it; the exact
price needs the reading in the step. And for a finite machine whose account prices
merges at least one, that account already charges every step that writes a permanent
record at least one; if the account also pays one unit per halving, it charges at least
the logarithm of the squeeze. Which events every account has to price: the merges,
on finite hardware, with every permanent-record write among them and revocation
as the only escape. Proved, with Landauer’s principle as the premise. The guess that
the priced events are exactly the permanent records is false. The pointer-observable
criterion is my conjecture for the event-selection question, and its strong consensus-
based necessity form is refuted.

Two results stand out as single pieces, one in the build and one in the model.

In the build, the sharpest thing I found is this: an integer-arithmetic check on the
CHSH outcome counters forces the zero-marginal NPA moment matrix to be positive
semidefinite, and the argument from the one to the other never once reaches for
physics. No Hilbert space, no operators, no observables anywhere in the chain. That
is the claim, and it is algebra you can follow line by line. Where I checked it is ch
sh_lassert_no_trap_implies_npa_psd, and the check is where I confirmed I
hadn’t smuggled physics in without noticing: Print Assumptions on it leans on
exactly three standard real-number axioms from Coq’s own library: Dedekind-reals
decidability (sig_forall_dec, sig_not_dec) and functional extensionality, and
nothing of mine. The substrate-level result below leans on even less.

In the model, at the substrate level, the diagonal rules out a correct decider when-


                                          192
The Thiele Machine                                            Devon Thiele • September 2026


ever its flip can be expressed inside the substrate and the instance supplies the fixed
point. The nat construction proves a family result: every candidate d gets its own in-
stance, and d fails on that instance’s predicate Pd . The VM version keeps its bounded
recurrence and representability premises. In L, a Turing-complete lambda calculus,
the recursion theorem is proved from the reduction rules, and the same diagonal is
Rice’s theorem (Section 10.4). Separately, the reachable-state collision shows the CSR
predicate is lost through forget. These are different claims. Sharing a vocabulary, or
a clean assumption report, doesn’t make them one.

All of this supports a structural account of computation. The A2 theorem covers any
system that follows its pricing rule. The irrecoverability theorem is about chosen
windows. Neither needs the concrete VM to be the whole subject, and neither says
every computation has to adopt this law. Calling one reading foundational is the
conjectural part of the argument.

Each of these results carries an explicit falsification condition, and they are gathered in
one place (Section 23): a zero-µ trace from uncertified to certified, a column-contractive
correlator with a non-PSD NPA matrix, a cost-rule-respecting CertificationSys
tem that certifies at total cost zero, a nat function that decides nat_admits for its
own substrate. Any one of them collapses the matching theorem. You can hunt for
any of them with a pencil, and the mechanised ones would stop the checker dead on
top of that. I hunted. I came up empty.

I do not want this to sound like I am declaring I am right. I want to push myself, and
after investigating everything, maybe the field too.

I built this. The definitions are here, the proofs are here, and the machine runs. If I
have claimed more than a theorem gives me, that sentence needs fixing. If a proof
fails, the proof needs fixing. I want to know where. I also want the larger question to
survive the inspection: what gets left out when a computation is described only by
the answer it returns? Certification gave me one place to get a grip on that question. I
intend to keep going.

A Things I had to learn first
I had been around computers my whole life and paid attention to people like John
Carmack on how graphics actually get made, but I came in with none of the formal
fields this work turned out to need. By the time the proofs compiled I had needed to
learn enough of half a dozen of them that I had to put down somewhere what those
fields had words for. This appendix is that place. None of it is the argument. It is what
I needed to be able to make it.

Everything below is what I understood after I went and read about it. If I have
anything wrong, tell me. I am not claiming to teach you these subjects. I am putting
down what I had to take from each of them to get this work to compile. If you already



                                           193
The Thiele Machine                                          Devon Thiele • September 2026


know one of these subsections, skip it.

A.1 Bits, bytes, and what a CPU sees
A bit is one digit that is either zero or one, the smallest fact a machine can hold.
Everything else is bits stacked up. Eight of them stacked make a byte, which lands
you any number from 0 to 255, and the first thing that genuinely surprised me is that
the same byte is also a letter: a convention called ASCII (American Standard Code
for Information Interchange) simply agrees to call 65 the letter A, 48 the digit 0, 32 a
space. Nothing in the byte knows which it is. The meaning is a convention laid on top
of the data, and which convention you read it through is your choice, not the byte’s.
Hold onto that, because it is the whole argument of this document shrunk down to
one byte: the data sits there inert, and everything that matters is in the structure you
read it through. I learned the byte part in February.

A word is a chunk of bits the CPU treats as one unit. The kernel math uses 64-bit
words (each word can hold any number from 0 to 264 − 1, about 18 quintillion). The
synthesised hardware uses 32-bit words (each word holds 0 to 232 − 1, about 4 billion).
A word is just a sequence of bits with a fixed width. The substrate does not care which
width.

A register is a single named storage slot inside the CPU that holds one word. The
51-opcode VM has 16 of them, named r0 through r15. Registers are the fastest place
to read or write a value. Memory is a longer list of word-sized slots, accessed by
index (the “address”). The 51-opcode VM has 128 words of memory. That is the entire
address space. The substrate has no address space.

The program counter (PC) is one specific register that tracks where in the program the
VM is. After every instruction the PC moves forward by one unless the instruction
explicitly says jump to a different address.

A stack pointer is a register that points to the top of a region of memory the program
uses for nested function calls. CALL pushes a return address there; RET pops it. In the
51-opcode VM, r15 is the stack pointer by convention. The convention is mine. The
substrate is not consulted.

A port is a named channel the 51-opcode VM uses to communicate with the outside
world. READ_PORT brings a value in, WRITE_PORT sends a value out. The kernel
does not model the outside world. It just charges µ for the communication and accepts
the value the channel encoding declares. The outside world is welcome to comply.
The substrate has no ports.

Modular arithmetic means counting wraps around at a maximum. If the maximum
is 16, then 17 wraps to 1 and 19 wraps to 3. “Modulo 16” or “mod 16” means “wrap
at 16.” The 51-opcode VM wraps register indices modulo 16 and memory indices
modulo 128. If you write r17, the kernel treats that as r1.



                                          194
The Thiele Machine                                              Devon Thiele • September 2026


Bitwise operations work on the bits of a value rather than treating the value as a
number. Bitwise AND of two bits is 1 only when both bits are 1. Bitwise OR is 1 when
either is 1. Bitwise XOR (exclusive or, sometimes written ⊕) is 1 exactly when the two
bits differ. The bitwise version of these does the operation in parallel for every bit
position. Bitwise-AND of two 64-bit words gives a 64-bit word. Bit i of the result is
the AND of bit i of each input. The bits do not consult each other. The substrate does
not see bits.

A shift moves the bits of a value left or right. Left shift by 3 (sometimes written x ≪ 3)
moves every bit three positions to the left, which is the same as multiplying by 23 = 8.
Right shift divides by a power of 2. Same idea, opposite direction.

Popcount is the count of 1-bits in a value. Popcount of the byte 11010100 is 4. Some-
times called the Hamming weight; the two names refer to the same thing. Pick one.

Hexadecimal is base 16. Each “digit” is one of 0 through 9 or A through F, where
A means 10 and F means 15. Hex is convenient for working with bytes because
every two hex digits make one byte. Numbers prefixed with 0x are hex: 0xFF is 255,
0xCAFEEACE is the constant unlock key in the Logic Engine accumulator (the Logic
Engine is the machine’s on-chip formula checker; Section 5 builds it). It had to be
something.

A trap, in the hardware sense, means the machine routes execution to a fixed address
instead of advancing normally. It is the hardware equivalent of “this went wrong,
go to the error handler.” The 51-opcode VM traps LASSERT length mismatches to
address 0xF00 (LASSERT is the logic-assertion opcode; Section 12 builds it).

An ISA (instruction set architecture) is the list of all instructions the CPU recognises,
plus their formats and costs. Each instruction is identified by an opcode (a numeric tag
the hardware decodes to choose which behaviour to run). The kernel has 51 opcodes.
The substrate has none. The substrate has a step rule.

A.2 The notation the formal parts use
I don’t think in these symbols. The formal statements in this book were written in
standard notation by the models and checked in Coq, and I had to learn to read them
the same way you might be learning now. Here is what each one means, in plain
words, so you never have to take a symbol on faith.

N is the natural numbers: 0, 1, 2, 3, and so on. Z is the integers, which extend the
natural numbers with negatives: . . . , −2, −1, 0, 1, 2, . . .. R is the real numbers, all the
                                                                                 √
way along the number line including fractions and irrationals like π and 2.

∈ means “is an element of.” x ∈ S reads “x is in S.”

⊆ means “is a subset of” (every element of the left side is also in the right). ⊊ means
strict subset: a subset but not the whole thing.


                                             195
The Thiele Machine                                                 Devon Thiele • September 2026


∪ is set union, the set containing everything in either side. \ is set difference: A \ B is
everything in A that is not in B.

× between two sets is the Cartesian product: A × B is the set of all ( a, b) pairs with
a ∈ A and b ∈ B. Functions from a set A to a set B are written A → B.

For a finite set S, |S| means the number of elements in S. For a real number x, | x |
means the absolute value of x (its distance from zero).

∑i∈T f (i ) is the sum of f (i ) taken over every i in T. The big sigma is the summation
symbol.

∆x means “the change in x,” usually the difference between a final and an initial value
of x.

⇒ is logical implication. P ⇒ Q means: if P is true, then Q is true. ⇐⇒ is the
biconditional, also called “if and only if.” P ⇐⇒ Q means both P ⇒ Q and Q ⇒ P.

∀ is “for every.” ∃ is “there exists.” ∀ x. P( x ) reads “for every x, P( x ) is true.” ∃ x. P( x )
reads “there is some x that makes P( x ) true.”

⟨ X ⟩ around a random variable X means the expected value, the long-run average
value of X. I picked up this notation reading physics papers about Bell tests.

◦ between two functions is composition: ( f ◦ g)( x ) = f ( g( x )), first apply g, then
apply f . The same symbol appears in category theory for arrow composition.

⊕ in this document is bitwise XOR. ⊗ in this document has exactly one meaning: the
MORPH_TENSOR operator of Section 12, concatenation of two coupling lists into
one. Physics writes the same symbol for the tensor product of quantum operators;
that sense never appears on these pages, because Section 18 does its quantum work in
plain integer arithmetic.

A T is the transpose of a matrix A (rows and columns swapped). In is the n × n identity
matrix, which has 1 on the diagonal and 0 everywhere else.

A.3 Coq, in just enough detail
Coq is a proof assistant. It is a programming language. The type system is so ex-
pressive that you can write proofs as programs and have the compiler check whether
they are valid. Section 21 has the longer build, including why I trust it. Here is the
vocabulary that runs through the rest of the document.

A Theorem in Coq is a named statement followed by a proof. Lemma is the same
construct, conventionally used for intermediate results. Definition introduces a named
value (a number, a function, a record, anything). Inductive defines a type with a
finite list of named constructors, the only ways to build a value of that type. Coq’s
natural numbers, for example, are an inductive type with two constructors: zero, and
“successor of another natural number.” Two constructors, infinitely many naturals.

                                               196
The Thiele Machine                                              Devon Thiele • September 2026


A tactic is a step inside a Coq proof. The tactics that appear in this document include:
induction (prove the base case, then prove that if the statement holds at step n it holds
at step n + 1), destruct (split a hypothesis into its cases), exact (provide the literal proof
term), reflexivity (prove x = x), simpl (simplify the goal by computing), lra (linear
real arithmetic; closes goals that are linear inequalities over the reals), nra (nonlinear
real arithmetic; handles polynomial inequalities), and psatz (uses Positivstellensatz
certificates; it shows a polynomial is non-negative by writing it as a sum of squares). I
learned each of them by needing it.

Qed is the keyword that ends a proof. It asks Coq to check the entire chain. If Coq
accepts the proof, the theorem is proved.

Admitted is the keyword for “I am claiming this without proof.” This is what I am
not doing anywhere in the active proof tree. Every Admitted in the kernel would be a
bug. Section 22 explains what zero Admitted means. And what it does not.

A Hypothesis (a Coq keyword inside a Section) is a named premise: a statement taken
as given without proof. Hypotheses surface as ∀-quantified premises in the conclusion.
Hypothesis appears only as Section-local premises: in the physical-units calibration
(Section 19, the load-bearing example), the thermodynamic and quantum probe files,
the hardware fault model, and a few interpreter and substrate sections. Each is closed
into a theorem argument when its Section ends. An Axiom is the global version: a
top-level claim accepted without proof. The kernel tier has zero project-local Axiom
declarations. The policy is bookkeeping discipline, not faith.

Print Assumptions is the Coq command that lists the global axioms a given theorem
ultimately depends on. Running it on the kernel tier shows only standard Coq stdlib
axioms (functional extensionality, classical logic for the reals). No project-local axiom
appears anywhere in those dependency trees. One thing it does not show: a premise
written into the theorem’s own statement. Those stay in the type, and “closed under
the global context” does not make them go away. Whether they hold for the case you
care about is something you check by reading the statement.

A total function in Coq always returns a value. Coq requires every function definition
to be total. vm_apply is total. Every state-and-instruction pair has a defined result.
Including the error case.

“Parametric in K” means the proof works for any value of K. Register, memory, and
table sizes are explicit constants in the implementation. Changing them requires
rebuilding the affected definitions and checking their representation obligations. A
proof about the abstract state alone does not establish that every size choice fits a
physical target.




                                             197
The Thiele Machine                                               Devon Thiele • September 2026


A.4 Category theory: the part I needed beyond Section 13
Section 13 builds Category, Object, Morphism, Composition, Identity, and Associativ-
ity from scratch. That build is enough for the categorical-separation argument inside
this machine. Here is what I had to look up beyond that.

A functor is a structure-preserving map from one category to another. Given two
categories C and D , a functor F from C to D sends each object of C to an object of
D . It sends each arrow f : A → B in C to an arrow F ( f ) : F ( A) → F ( B) in D . It
respects composition (F ( g ◦ f ) = F ( g) ◦ F ( f )) and identity (F (id A ) = idF( A) ). Think
of a functor as a translation rule between two categorical worlds that keeps the laws
intact.

An initial object in a category has exactly one morphism into each object. Here thi
ele_trace_fold_initial proves that there is exactly one way to evaluate plain
lists of instructions into a target once you pick its starting point. That is a statement
about instruction lists. It doesn’t make the VM’s state space initial under certification-
preserving state maps. Those maps might not exist at all. When somebody supplies
them, their uniqueness on reachable states is a separate theorem.

An embedding is a functor that throws nothing away. That is the whole point of
putting the word here: the classical projection of a VM state loses things on the way
out, and an embedding is the move that does not. Faithful is the precise version of
“loses nothing on arrows”: if two arrows between the same pair of objects in C land
on the same arrow under F, they were already the same arrow, so F never quietly
fuses two distinct things into one. That is injectivity on each hom-set, checked one
pair of objects at a time. Conservative is the version for invertibility: if F ( f ) has an
inverse over in D , then f already had one back in C , so F cannot manufacture an
isomorphism that was not there. (An isomorphism is just an arrow with an inverse.)
These are different properties, and neither implies the other. The inclusion of the
one-object category of the monoid (N, +) into the one-object category of (Z, +) is
faithful (distinct natural numbers stay distinct integers) but not conservative: 1 has
no inverse in N, yet its image in Z does. Conversely, the functor from the one-object
category of the group Z to the trivial one-object group is conservative (every arrow
in the trivial group is already invertible, so nothing needs reflecting) but not faithful:
it fuses every integer onto the same single arrow. Both properties stop a map from
inventing structure it shouldn’t have, but they guard against different inventions, and
neither stands in for the other. An embedding needs faithfulness specifically.

A monoidal product is a way of combining two objects, or two arrows, into one. In
the category of sets, the Cartesian product A × B is one. A full monoidal product
also comes with a unit and with laws that make the combining behave; that whole
bundle is what mathematicians mean by a monoidal structure. MORPH_TENSOR is
tensor-shaped, and I want to be exact about how far that goes. Take two morphisms
whose source and target regions don’t overlap. Pack them into a single morphism


                                              198
The Thiele Machine                                          Devon Thiele • September 2026


whose coupling is the concatenation. If the regions do overlap, the operation refuses.
It also refuses unless the combined regions already exist as modules. Coq checks that
local behavior. It does not build the full monoidal category with all its coherence maps.
The symbol ⊗ below names this operation.

Yoneda’s lemma is where the early categorical engine clicked for me, the night I
stopped borrowing other people’s runtimes. Nothing in the formal development uses
it. It doesn’t show up in any theorem’s dependencies. The checked results rest on the
category, morphism, and composition structures defined in the repository.

A.5 Bell-test vocabulary you will meet in Section 18
Section 18 builds the CHSH game from scratch. A few foundational terms thread
through that section that I will not rebuild each time.

A correlation between two outcomes is the average value of their product. If Alice’s
outcome a is ±1 and Bob’s outcome b is ±1, the correlation is ⟨ ab⟩, which lies between
−1 and +1. A correlation of +1 means they always match. −1 means they always dis-
agree. 0 means there is no average correlation. A correlator is one specific correlation
in a labelled setting: for example, E00 is the correlation when Alice’s setting is 0 and
Bob’s setting is 0.

The marginal of a joint distribution on ( a, b) is the distribution on a alone, with b
averaged out. Zero-marginal means Alice’s marginal gives ⟨ a⟩ = 0 and Bob’s gives
⟨b⟩ = 0. Each outcome is +1 or −1 equally often, on average. The Section 18 derivation
works in this symmetric slice.

A local hidden variable model is a classical-physics-style theory where Alice’s and
Bob’s outcomes are determined by a shared random variable λ they agreed on before
the game started, plus their own measurement setting. “Local” means no communica-
tion during the game. “Hidden” means λ need not be directly observable. “Variable”
because λ is random. Bell’s theorem says no local hidden variable model can produce
certain correlations that quantum mechanics can.

A no-signaling correlation is one where Alice’s marginal does not depend on Bob’s
setting, and Bob’s marginal does not depend on Alice’s setting. You cannot learn Bob’s
setting by looking only at Alice’s outputs. Not even in principle. Most correlations in
this book (classical, quantum, and the PR-box below) are no-signaling.

Quantum-realizable, in this document, means “compatible with some quantum-
mechanical state-and-measurement description.” The NPA framework (Navascués-
Pironio-Acín) gives a hierarchy of tests for it, each one positive-semidefiniteness of
a moment matrix. Every level is necessary. No single level is sufficient in general.
Section 18 builds the first level and part of the next. The theorems here prove PSD of
those matrices. They don’t prove quantum realizability.

The PR-box (Popescu-Rohrlich box) is a hypothetical correlation that wins the CHSH


                                          199
The Thiele Machine                                           Devon Thiele • September 2026


game with probability 1, achieving |S| = 4. It is no-signaling, but stronger than any-
thing quantum mechanics allows. Nature does not provide it. It is the standard coun-
terexample to the false claim “any no-signaling correlation is quantum-realizable.”

The Cauchy-Schwarz inequality is the basic inner-product inequality |⟨ x, y⟩| ≤ ∥ x ∥ ·
∥y∥. It reads: the inner product of two vectors is bounded by the product of their
lengths. It is one of the workhorse algebraic moves in the Section 18 derivation.
                                      √
The Tsirelson bound is the value 2 2 ≈ 2.828, the largest CHSH score |S| that any
quantum-mechanical strategy can achieve. The classical bound is 2. The PR-box bound
is 4. Tsirelson sits strictly between classical and PR-box. Nature picked one of these
three.

The elliptope, named in Section 24 and Section 25, is a set of correlators: the ones that
can be filled out into a positive-semidefinite matrix. In the usual vector or quantum
reading it means something physical. The Coq development here proves the real-
matrix statement and what follows from it. It doesn’t prove a full physical realization
theorem. The proof in Section 18 covers one slice of the set, the one with zero marginals
and a fixed completion.

A.6 Hardware and synthesis vocabulary
Section 16 builds the scaffolding stack (Coq kernel, OCaml runner, Kami RTL) carefully.
Here is the synthesis-tooling vocabulary that Section 16’s third subsection uses without
expanding each tool.

A hardware description language (HDL) is a programming language for describing
circuits. Bluespec SystemVerilog and Verilog are two HDLs. Bluespec is higher-level:
you describe rules and the compiler figures out the gate-level details. Verilog is lower-
level: you describe gates and wires more directly. Verilog RTL specifically means
“register-transfer level” Verilog, which describes how data moves between registers
each clock cycle. It is what synthesis tools consume. They do not eat Bluespec.

Synthesis is the process of compiling a hardware description down to actual logic gates
(the building blocks of chips). yosys is an open-source synthesis tool. After synthesis,
place-and-route assigns the gates to physical positions on a chip and connects them.
nextpnr-xilinx is the place-and-route tool for the Xilinx FPGAs this design targets.
The last stretch is producing a bitstream, the binary file the FPGA reads to configure
itself, and prjxray does it in two moves: fasm2frames packs the routed design into
configuration frames, then xc7frames2bit writes the frames out as the bitstream. Four
steps, four tools, one chip.

An FPGA (field-programmable gate array) is a chip you can program after manufac-
turing. The gates are physical. The connections between them are configurable. An
ASIC (application-specific integrated circuit) is the non-programmable version: gates
fixed at manufacturing time for one specific design.



                                          200
The Thiele Machine                                            Devon Thiele • September 2026


A LUT (look-up table) is the basic logic primitive in an FPGA: a small memory that
maps a fixed-length list of inputs to an output, programmed at configuration time
to implement any small boolean function. A flip-flop is a one-bit memory cell that
holds its value across clock cycles. A carry chain is specialised wiring for fast addition.
Distributed RAM is RAM built from LUTs. Block RAM is dedicated RAM blocks on
the chip. DSP slices are specialised hardware for digital-signal-processing arithmetic.
The synthesised K325T design uses none of them. yosys runs with -nodsp, so the
multiplier in the multi-cycle witness checker (Section 16) is built out of LUTs. Put on
DSP slices instead, the same multiplier couldn’t get through the open-source place-
and-route in time. It computes the same thing either way. The choice is about what
the toolchain can place and route, not about correctness.

A JSON netlist is the gate-level circuit serialised in a JSON text file (JSON is a standard
text format for structured data). A VCD waveform is a Value Change Dump, a record
of every signal’s value at every time step in a simulation. The name describes the file.
iverilog is an open-source Verilog simulator. It runs the design and produces a VCD
output you can inspect.

A bisimulation is a precise notion of “two systems behave the same.” You pick a
relation between the states of one system and the states of the other. Related states
have to agree on whatever you chose to observe, and whenever one of them takes a
step, the other has a matching step that lands in related states again. That is looser than
“same output state, field by field.” Field-by-field equality is one particular, stronger
contract. Section 16 says exactly which observations and transitions the hardware
relation covers. A parity test is the empirical cousin: run two implementations on
the same battery of inputs and check they agree, a correspondence earned by testing
rather than by proof. The kernel-to-OCaml boundary is closed that way, and the
document says so where it happens.

A.7 Other terms I use without rebuilding
Provenance, in software, means the recorded chain of where an object came from. A
morphism’s provenance is the specific sequence of MORPH and COMPOSE instruc-
tions that built it. You can replay the chain.

Simulation, in computer science, means one system imitating another’s input-output
behaviour. A Turing machine simulates a Thiele Machine when it produces the same
outputs on the same inputs, by writing the whole Thiele state out across its tape and
grinding through it. That is real, and it matters, and it is exactly the thing people reach
for when they want to wave the whole question away: if a tape can stand in for the
state, what was the difference? The difference is that the tape carries a picture of the
state, not the state, the way a photograph of a fire keeps you exactly as warm as any
other photograph. The cost ledger and the A2 rule live in the step relation, not in
the bytes, and an encoding copies the bytes. Simulating the substrate is not being the
substrate. A Turing machine can be one, though: build its transition table so the rule


                                            201
The Thiele Machine                                            Devon Thiele • September 2026


is its own step rule, and it is an instance, not a simulation. What no machine can do is
make a simulated rule binding just by writing it down on tape.

Projection, in mathematics, is a function that drops information on purpose. The
classical projection of a VM state is the one that does the dropping I keep pointing at:
the strict shadow throws away µ, certification, and the morphism graph, and keeps
registers, memory, and the program counter. Other windows keep more: forget also
keeps µ, and shadow_proj keeps six fields and drops the graph. The three the strict
shadow keeps are the three a Turing machine already has, which is the whole reason
the projection lands you back in ordinary computation. The three it drops are the three
that were doing the new work. Nothing about the projection is wrong; it is a perfectly
good function. It just costs you everything that made the state more than a tape.

Quotient, in mathematics, is the operation of declaring two things the same when they
agree on what you decided to look at, and then working with the lumped-together
classes instead of the originals. The classical projection is exactly that kind of move:
any two VM states that match on registers, memory, and the program counter get
swept into the same class, even when their µ, their certification, and their morphism
graph were completely different. So the projection is not just dropping fields one at a
time; it is gluing together states that the dropped fields kept apart. That is the precise
sense in which it loses information, and it is comforting that the math already had the
word sitting there waiting.

A kernel of a map is context-dependent. For a group or module homomorphism it
is the inverse image of the identity element; for the quotient induced by a projection
π, the relevant equivalence relation is s ∼ t exactly when π (s) = π (t). The classical
projection in this document uses that second quotient reading. “Kernel” wears three
different hats in this document and I would rather call that out than let it ambush
you: this mathematical usage; the Coq proof core, meaning the kernel-tier theorems
in Section 24, which is scaffolding I lean on, not the substrate itself; and the operating-
system kernel, which I imply at most by analogy and never actually use. Whenever
the word shows up, the surrounding sentence tells you which one is meant.

Embedding is a function that preserves structure and is injective. Faithful embedding
additionally preserves all the structure-relevant distinctions.

An injection is a function where no two inputs map to the same output. A surjection is
a function whose image covers every possible output. Their negations are noninjective
and nonsurjective.

Decidable, in computer science, means there is an algorithm that always halts with
a yes-or-no answer. Keep the question and the algorithm apart. The predicate is the
mathematical claim P( x ). The decision procedure is the algorithm, and it only decides
P once there is a proof that its answer matches. The integer-only Z-arithmetic check
in CHSH_LASSERT always halts with a Boolean. Its soundness theorem says a pass


                                           202
The Thiele Machine                                             Devon Thiele • September 2026


means the stated real-matrix predicate holds.

Polynomial complexity: an algorithm is polynomial-time if there is some power k such
that the algorithm’s running time on inputs of size n is at most a constant times nk .
Polynomial-space is the analogous concept for memory used. The class P is decision
problems solvable in polynomial time. The class NP is decision problems where, given
a candidate solution, a polynomial-time algorithm can check whether the solution is
correct. The famous P-vs-NP question asks whether finding is fundamentally harder
than checking. Nobody has proved either direction. The predicates error_free_pr
eservation_budget and positive_input_certification_budget only con-
strain traces chosen separately for each input. They do not express these complexity
classes.

Determinant: a number computed from a square matrix that says whether the matrix
is singular (zero determinant
                    !          means the matrix collapses some direction to zero). For a
                a b
2 × 2 matrix           the determinant is ad − bc. For larger matrices, you can compute
                c d
it by “expansion along a row”: pick a row, for each entry take the determinant of the
submatrix you get by deleting that entry’s row and column (with alternating signs),
multiply by the entry, and sum. The recursion bottoms out at the 2 × 2 case above.
Section 18 walks the explicit calculations for the 3 × 3 cases the argument needs.

Schur complement: a way of turning  ! a question about a block matrix into a question
                              A B
about a smaller matrix. If             is a block matrix where A is invertible, the Schur
                              C D
complement of A is D − CA−1 B. The fact the argument uses: for a symmetric real block
matrix whose pivot A is positive definite, the whole matrix is positive-semidefinite
exactly when the Schur complement is. Invertible alone is not enough. The pivot has
to be positive definite. The Section 18 build uses this for the 4 × 4 block of the specified
moment matrix.

A principal submatrix is the square subblock you get by picking a subset of indices
and keeping only the rows and columns at those indices. A standard result is that
a matrix is positive-semidefinite if and only if every principal submatrix is positive-
semidefinite. Every one. Section 18 relies on this.

A.8 The axioms (A1 through A6)
The Thiele Machine has a small numbered list of substrate-level axioms. They appear
by name (“A2,” “A3,” . . . ) throughout the document and the formalization. The list
below is what each name means, where it lives in the kernel, and what its conclusion
is. The axioms are substrate-level; the kernel is scaffolding-level; the list bridges the
two.

The kernel keeps two parallel axiom-numbering systems because two parallel sub-
strate abstractions are formalised. The AbstractCertMachine record carries A1


                                            203
The Thiele Machine                                            Devon Thiele • September 2026


(the cert indicator exists, by record existence) and A3 (only cert-setters flip it, the
acm_preserve field). A2 (cert starts unset) and A4 (cert-setters cost ≥ 1) live
alongside the record, not in it: A2 is the acm_cert M s0 = false hypothesis to
abstract_nfi, A4 is the standalone lemma cert_addr_setter_cost_pos. The
consolidated CertificationSystem record folds the cost-on-certification seman-
tic content into a single field kept by the name A2 (the load-bearing piece). The Q
uantitativeCertificationSystem extension adds A3 and A5, with A4 in the
QuantitativeCertificationSystem_full record and A6 a theorem hypothe-
sis; together they lift the cost-floor result from ≥ 1 to ≥ K for an arbitrary threshold
K. The two numbering systems agree on the cost-on-certification semantic content;
they disagree on which axiom carries that content (A4 in the AbstractCertMachine list,
A2 in the consolidated CertificationSystem list). When the rest of this document says
“A2,” it means the cost-on-certification rule, in the consolidated CertificationSystem
sense.

A1 (state includes a certification indicator).
An AbstractCertMachine record bundles a state type S, a step function acm_step,
and a boolean cert indicator acm_cert : S → bool. A1 is the structural require-
ment that this indicator exists at all. In Coq, A1 is enforced by the record type itself.
There is no way to hand Coq an AbstractCertMachine value with acm_cert left
blank; it reads the field as missing and declines to build the record, the way a clerk
declines a form with an empty box. A1 is discharged at type-check time.
A2 (cost-on-certification).
In the consolidated CertificationSystem record, Coq field cs_cert_costs.
Statement: for every state s and instruction i, if cs_cert s = false and cs_cert
(cs_step s i) = true, then cs_cost i ≥ 1. In words: any single step that
flips the certification predicate from no to yes must have positive cost. This is the load-
bearing axiom for No Free Insight. universal_nfi_any_substrate concludes
from A2 alone that every trace from uncertified to certified has total cost ≥ 1. Note: in
the AbstractCertMachine numbering, the same content appears as A4 (cert-setters cost
≥ 1). The AbstractCertMachine A2 is the separate precondition that the cert indicator
starts unset, which the consolidated system handles as a theorem hypothesis instead
of a numbered axiom.
A3 (cost bounds witness growth).
Coq field qcs_cost_bounds_witness on the record QuantitativeCertific
ationSystem. Statement: for every state s and instruction i, qcs_witness s +
cs_cost i ≥ qcs_witness (cs_step s i). In words: each instruction’s cost
is an upper bound on how much the system’s accumulated evidence (the “witness”)
can grow in that step. The cost is the speed-limit on evidence accumulation. (In
the AbstractCertMachine numbering, A3 is the field acm_preserve: non-cert-setter
instructions preserve the cert indicator. The two A3s are distinct properties on distinct
record types; the monograph’s A3 is the quantitative one above.)


                                           204
The Thiele Machine                                           Devon Thiele • September 2026


A4 (witness nondecreasing).
Coq field qcsf_witness_nondecreasing on the record QuantitativeCer
tificationSystem_full. Statement: for every s and i, qcs_witness s ≤
qcs_witness (cs_step s i). A3 and A4 point in opposite directions: A3 caps
how fast the witness can grow (w′ ≤ w + cost), A4 stops it from shrinking (w ≤ w′ ).
Neither implies the other. (w, cost, w′ ) = (1, 0, 0) satisfies A3 with non-negative cost
yet violates A4, and (0, 0, 1) does the reverse, so A4 is its own field, not a consequence
of A3. It is also idle: the ≥ K floor telescopes through A3, A5, and A6 alone and never
touches A4. The field is kept so downstream consumers that want witness monotonic-
ity in hand have it without re-deriving it; the consolidated QuantitativeCertifi
cationSystem record drops A4 entirely, carrying only A3 and A5.
A5 (certification requires witness).
Coq field qcs_cert_threshold_witness on QuantitativeCertificationSy
stem. Statement: cs_cert s = true → qcs_witness s ≥ qcs_threshold.
In words: certification cannot fire until the system has accumulated at least
qcs_threshold-worth of evidence. This is what lets the quantitative theorem push
the cost floor from ≥ 1 to ≥ K, where K is the threshold. Pay more, certify more.
A6 (witness initial zero).
The hypothesis that the run starts with zero witness, stated as a precondition on the
quantitative theorem rather than a record field. Without A6 the telescoping bound
becomes K − initial witness instead of K. Zero is a clean place to start.

A1 through A6, as the consolidated records state them, are independent of any specific
instruction set; the AbstractCertMachine variant is the one exception, typing its step
over the VM’s own instruction type. They are conditions on Coq record types; supply-
ing instances of those record types is the only way to invoke the corresponding theo-
rems. The 51-opcode VM supplies them by construction: the kernel builds two con-
crete CertificationSystem values for the VM (one for the csr_cert_addr chan-
nel, one for the vm_certified channel), each discharging A2 from the existing no_
free_certification kernel theorems. A separate substrate-level abstraction (the
Substrate typeclass) carries a related but distinct field Substrate.mu_monotone
stating that the cost ledger never decreases on a step. This is a different, more general
property than A2. A2 says the cert transition costs ≥ 1. Monotonicity only says the
ledger never goes down. Neither is stated as implying the other. Both hold on the
51-opcode VM, where every instruction adds non-negative cost and the cert transition
adds at least 1. Two locks on the same door, by different keys.

That is the vocabulary. The rest of the document uses these words without rebuilding
them.




                                           205
The Thiele Machine                                          Devon Thiele • September 2026



Exact scope
The proved structural result is the record axis: a permanent record driven by a base
computation is a latch on an event; on finite hardware its write merges states; and the
VM’s named projections do not recover it. Certification is one instance, not a theorem
selecting the unique event every system must record.

Finite growing records decompose into threshold latches, but not in general into one
latch. Revocable and probabilistic records do not inherit the same uniqueness theo-
rem. The guest VM has a direct Rice reduction, numeric decoding, verified dynamic
dispatch, and semantic s-m-n specialization, but no internal recursion theorem: the
evaluator and numeric specializer are not guest-callable. The exact two-state calorime-
ter protocol also proves that population dynamics do not determine the Hamiltonian
scale. The formal ledger therefore assigns no intrinsic joule value to one unit of µ
without thermal and device premises.

The RFC 9162 verifier covers the iterative inclusion and consistency control flow and
examples, not collision resistance or signed-tree-head authenticity. The PCC checker
is a small role model; the RAM theorem covers addressed list memory; the reversible-
machine theorem covers arithmetic update cores; and the TPM theorem shows that
an unconstrained signature interface does not entail quote authenticity. The cost-
semantics results are local correspondences, not full cost-framework embeddings.

The twelve-event observer-map survey proves only its formal classifications and its
response to swapping the proliferating event. Its MAC-labelled counterexample de-
pends on the observer model when read as a claim about real systems. A separate
two-observer toggle game refutes the stronger claim that consensus, exact observation,
and coordinator-free evolution force permanent commits; adding durable observation
makes permanence immediate from the premise. Five narrow external consequences
reduce to known indistinguishability or durability arguments, and five stronger can-
didates lack the protocol or hardware semantics needed for proof. None yields a
new external result that both needs the record axis and is ready for use. The pointer
criterion is a conjecture, and this proposed necessity route to it is closed by the coun-
terexample.
